# Gap analysis — coverage verification of the 92 merged items (2026-11-01)

A second, skeptical pass re-grepped every merged item in §10 of the review against `chapters/*.md`, read the hits, and ruled **genuinely-missing** (nothing beyond passing mentions), **partially-covered** (one angle exists; the rest is missing) or **already-covered** (dropped). No item was already covered. Priorities below are the verifier's final call; `(was …)` marks a change from the lens proposal.


## G01 — Zero-overhead application telemetry: counters, gauges, latency histograms, shared-memory export and off-core scrapers

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#1, observability-depth#2, hft-domain#3
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-app) and central to operating a tick-to-trade system: Ch. 76 and Ch. 74 both assume per-stage histograms and counters are exported from an isolated busy-poll process but no chapter shows how to do it without a syscall, a format call or a cache-line fight.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part XII immediately after Ch. 71 (Zero-Overhead Logging), as its metrics sibling, before Ch. 72; renumber 72–76 with the existing script. A section inside Ch. 76 is too small: the chapter must build the shared-memory telemetry segment (counters/gauges/high-water marks laid out per hot thread on padded lines; per-stage HdrHistograms with interval-vs-cumulative snapshot semantics published under a seqlock — Ch. 35), the off-core scraper on a housekeeping core (Ch. 42) that reads without pulling the hot thread's lines, export to a TSDB/Prometheus-style endpoint from the scraper (never the hot process), and the measurements (per-increment cost, scraper-induced HITM, snapshot cost). This segment is also the substrate G02 (progress sequences), G05 (health queries) and G06 (readiness state) need, which strengthens the new-chapter case. Fold G04 in as the mental-model section on percentile math and a closing section on SLOs/alerts. Cross-reference from Ch. 74 §74.4.3 and Ch. 76 §76.2 instead of duplicating.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Recording-side building blocks exist (Ch. 30/31/33 cost model and padding; Ch. 3/App L HdrHistogram API). Missing and must be written: counter taxonomy (counter vs gauge vs high-water, reset-on-read vs monotonic), the shm telemetry segment layout and versioned snapshot protocol, histogram interval/cumulative semantics and hdr_add merging, the scraper design and its measured interference on the hot core, export/transport off-box, what a trading process must expose (per-feed liveness age, gap/drop counters per layer, queue depth/high-water from existing ring indices, per-stage latency, order-session ack-latency/reject/throttle counts), and the pitfalls (formatting or syscalls on the hot thread, contended global counters, scraping the hot thread's own lines).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 31-multithreading-concurrency-foundations.md §31.3, §31.4 (per-thread padded counters aggregated by a monitoring thread — one bullet)
  - 33-false-sharing-thread-safety-anomalies.md §33.3 (padded vs unpadded counter HITM measurement)
  - 30-cpp-memory-model-atomics.md §30.2, §30.3 (relaxed fetch_add cost, contention collapse)
  - 03-microbenchmarking-done-right.md §3.4.3 and appendix-L-benchmark-harness.md L.2 (HdrHistogram record/read-out in a benchmark)
  - 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.2 (the 'what to observe' list, no mechanism), §76.4.1 (trend dashboard phrase)
  - 50-inter-process-communication.md §50.4.2 (shm segment header with PIDs for liveness)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il across chapters/*.md: Prometheus, StatsD, OpenTelemetry, Grafana, exporter, scrape, sidecar, 'telemetry segment', hdr_add, 'interval histogram', 'high-water'/'high water', 'time-series', 'stats thread' = 0 files. 'telemetry' only Ch. 64 (HPCC in-network telemetry) and Ch. 70 (DPU timestamps); 'gauge' only Ch. 17 ('cycle-rate gauge', unrelated); 'metrics' Ch. 2 (IPC), Ch. 31, Ch. 65. Opened the hits: Ch. 76 §76.2 is a diagram plus three bullets asserting production observation is '~free' and listing 'per-stage HdrHistogram ... counters: drops, gaps, queue depths, restarts' with no mechanism for recording, snapshotting or exporting; §76.4.1 'Track the trend ... (a dashboard)' is one phrase. Ch. 31 §31.4 has exactly one bullet ('a monitoring thread sums them periodically ... applies to metrics, P&L accumulation, histograms') and §31.3 measures sharded vs shared counters; Ch. 30 §30.3 measures relaxed fetch_add uncontended vs contended; Ch. 33 §33.3 measures padded vs unpadded per-thread counters with HITM. Ch. 3 §3.4.3 and App L.2 show hdr_init/hdr_record_value/hdr_value_at_percentile in a benchmark loop only — no interval vs cumulative, no reset, no merge, no export. Ch. 50 §50.4.2 mentions a segment header carrying PIDs 'for liveness'. Ch. 71 §71.4.3 mentions 'periodic stats' output via std::println. Nothing anywhere shows a telemetry segment, a snapshot protocol, an off-core scraper, or the per-increment/scraper-interference measurement.

## G02 — Liveness beyond heartbeats: progress-based stall detection, consumer lag, the watchdog chain and hung-process forensics before restart

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#2, debug-diag#6
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-app) and the most dangerous silent failure of a pinned busy-poll feed handler — it burns 100% CPU and keeps heartbeating while making no progress — is exactly what the existing Ch. 74 mechanism cannot detect.
- **Refined placement:** Expand Ch. 74 §74.4.3 from three bullets into a full technique section 'Liveness via progress, not heartbeats' (publish last-consumed-input and last-published-output sequences in the shm header; supervisor computes consumer lag = producer_seq − consumer_seq; stall predicate = lag growing while consumer flat; idle-vs-stuck disambiguated by the feed's own cadence — Ch. 53/54; the watchdog chain: application heartbeat → supervisor → systemd WatchdogSec/sd_notify → /dev/watchdog or softdog → kill-switch), and add a new §74.4.6 'Diagnose before you restart' (the supervisor's ordered protocol on a missed heartbeat: read-only /proc/<tid>/stat state and wchan, schedstat, ctxt switches; then SIGQUIT/gcore to a non-hot disk per §74.4.5; then kill — all time-budgeted against the recovery tail of §74.3). Add a §74.3 measurement (stall-detect latency vs false-positive rate for heartbeat vs progress) and a §74.5 pitfall ('a heartbeat written from inside the poll loop proves the loop spins, not that it processes'). No new chapter; the G05 control-plane section can share the same shm header.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Missing: progress/lag-based stall detection, idle-vs-stuck using exchange heartbeat/sequence cadence, backlog/high-water monitoring from existing ring indices, the kernel/systemd watchdog mechanics, a hang taxonomy on an isolated core with its /proc fingerprint (spinning R-state vs blocked S/D with wchan vs RT-throttled vs T-state), and the capture-evidence-then-restart protocol. Cross-link Ch. 45 §45.5 (RT throttling as a hang cause) and Ch. 5 §5.4.2.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.4.3 (heartbeat liveness, restart policy, watchdog phrase), §74.3 (Detect row), §74.5 ('Heartbeat that only catches exits'), §74.6 exercise 5
  - 50-inter-process-communication.md §50.4.2 (segment header PIDs for liveness), §50.5 (orphaned segment detection)
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.4.2 (core-dump post-mortem), §5.4.3 (production tracing), §5.5 (gdb attach hazard)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: 'progress counter', 'progress sequence', 'stall detect', 'consumer lag', '/dev/watchdog', softdog, hung_task, wchan, SIGSTOP, 'make progress', deadman/'dead-man', schedstat, getrusage = 0 files. 'missed heartbeat' only Ch. 74. 'heartbeat' in 45/46/51/73/74/App K — opened: Ch. 45 and App C mention watchdog only as kernel threads not to starve / nmi_watchdog=0; Ch. 46/51 are FIX/TCP keep-alive heartbeats; Ch. 73 is a teaser. 'liveness' in Ch. 35 (seqlock retry bound), Ch. 50 §50.4.2 (header PIDs 'for liveness — Ch. 74') and §50.5 (orphaned segments), Ch. 65 (transport liveness). 'queue depth' only Ch. 76 §76.2 diagram. Read Ch. 74 §74.3 (Detect row: heartbeat timeout/SIGCHLD; 'tune the heartbeat for detect-vs-false-positive'), §74.4.3 (three bullets: shm heartbeat slot catches hangs, restart policy with backoff/cap/escalation, 'a hardware or kernel watchdog (and an application watchdog)' as one phrase), §74.5 'Heartbeat that only catches exits', §74.6 exercise 5 (busy-loop hang caught by heartbeat timeout). The heartbeat is process-liveness: nothing distinguishes a loop that spins from one that processes, nothing compares producer vs consumer sequences, nothing disambiguates idle from stuck using feed cadence, nothing on /dev/watchdog or systemd WatchdogSec, and no diagnostic step before restart. Ch. 5 §5.4.2/§5.4.3/§5.5 cover core-dump post-mortem, eBPF production tracing and 'never attach gdb to the live hot path' but not a supervisor-driven /proc fingerprint or gcore-before-kill on heartbeat miss.

## G03 — Feed and session health signals: silence vs quiet market, A/B skew, book invariants, venue heartbeats, stale-data kill and source failover beyond A/B

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#5, resilience-ha#9
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-app); quoting on a frozen or crossed book is the silent-loss failure Ch. 54 cannot see, and the book already points readers to a FIX-heartbeat treatment that does not exist.
- **Refined placement:** New §54.4.4 in Ch. 54, 'Feed liveness, data-quality invariants and session health beyond gaps', after §54.4.3; extend the §54.2 model with liveness as the fourth signal (a dead line produces no gap) and add a §54.5 pitfall ('silence is not a quiet market'). Contents: exchange heartbeat/sequence-advance cadence per channel as the liveness reference, per-channel last-packet and per-instrument last-update age with thresholds adapted to session phase and halts, A/B arrival skew and which line wins arbitration as a leading indicator, cheap decode-time book invariants (crossed/locked book, negative size, impossible level — cross-message, complementing Ch. 72 §72.4.3's per-message ranges), venue session heartbeats/TestRequest and sequence-reset handling on the order session, the stale-data kill (pull quotes when book age exceeds X µs — the market-data counterpart of Ch. 74's kill-switch), and source failover beyond A/B (secondary venue feed/consolidated feed, warm standby handler — Ch. 46). Fix the three dangling 'FIX heartbeats — Ch. 53' pointers (Ch. 38 §38.1, Ch. 46 §46.4.2, Ch. 51 §51.4.1) to point here, and add a one-paragraph FIX session layer (Heartbeat/TestRequest/ResendRequest) to App J.3.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The gap-triggered half is genuinely developed; the time-, rate- and invariant-triggered half and the session layer are absent. Keep the section's measurement: time-to-detect a silent line vs false-positive rate across session phases, and the hot-path cost of the invariant checks (should be a few predicted branches).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 54-reliable-multicast-feed-recovery.md §54.2, §54.4.1–§54.4.3, §54.5 (gap-triggered staleness, recovery escalation)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.2.3, §53.4.3 (A/B arbitration, reorder window + timeout)
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.4.3 (per-message price/quantity range checks — adjacent)
  - 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md §58.4.1 (continuous PTP offset alerting — the one health signal developed)
  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.3, K.6 (physical-path failover, not feed-source failover)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: 'crossed book', 'locked book', staleness, 'last update'/'last-update', TestRequest/'test request', 'silent feed', 'dead line'/'dead feed', 'expected rate', 'stale-data', 'no activity', silence = 0 files. 'quiet market' only Ch. 46 §46.1 (warm vs cold, not feed liveness). 'stale' in Ch. 53/54 is exclusively gap-triggered (mark instrument stale on a sequence discontinuity). 'FIX heartbeat' hits Ch. 46 §46.4.2 and Ch. 51 §51.4.1 both say 'FIX heartbeats — Ch. 53', and Ch. 38 §38.1 says 'FIX session logic — Ch. 53', but grep heartbeat 53-*.md = 0 and App J has no heartbeat/TestRequest/logon/session-layer content — dangling pointers confirmed. 'failover' hits are process failover (Ch. 74), connectivity failover (App K.3 microwave→fiber, K.6 redundant cross-connects) and simulation (Ch. 76); nothing on switching market-data sources. Read Ch. 54 §54.2 (three channels, all keyed on sequence discontinuity), §54.3 (gap rate), §54.4.1 (gap vs reorder), §54.4.3 (stale-on-gap policy, kill-switch escalation on recovery failure), §54.5; Ch. 53 §53.2.3/§53.4.3 (A/B take-first, bounded reorder window + timeout — still requires a later sequence to arrive); Ch. 72 §72.4.3 validates per-message price/quantity ranges at the wire gate but no cross-message book invariant (crossed/locked). Ch. 58 §58.4.1 does alert on PTP offset. Nothing time-, rate- or invariant-triggered exists for the feed.

## G04 — Tick-to-trade SLOs, alert design, and percentile math for dashboards (merge histograms, never average percentiles)

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#6, observability-depth#6
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-app) and a direct corollary of the book's north-star: a desk that averages per-host p99.9s on its dashboard has reinstated the mean Ch. 1 spends a chapter denouncing, and nothing in the book warns them.
- **Refined placement:** Inside the G01 telemetry chapter: percentile math as its mental-model core (percentiles do not average across hosts, symbols or windows; merge histograms with hdr_add and recompute; interval vs cumulative; what 'p99.9 over the last second' means and the minimum samples per window; a worked example where the averaged p99.9 is off by ~10× from the merged one) and a closing technique section 'From metrics to SLOs and alerts' (SLIs: wire-to-wire and per-stage p99.9 per venue/shard, drops/gaps, unacked-order age, reject rate, PTP offset; SLOs budgeted per session phase because the open is a different regime; alert on symptoms with multi-window conditions, hysteresis and grouping; add 'alerting on means / paging on every gap' as a pitfall). If the G01 chapter is not adopted, split: §3.4.5 in Ch. 3 ('Percentiles in production: aggregation and windows') with a pointer from Ch. 1 §1.5, and §76.4.4 in Ch. 76 ('From metrics to alerts'). Prefer the G01 home to keep Ch. 3 benchmark-focused.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The percentile-merge lesson is the must-have (it is Ch. 1's thesis applied to dashboards, and the default aggregation silently reintroduces the mean); the SLO/alert-design half can be compact and should stay operational rather than SRE-textbook.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 01-the-latency-mindset.md §1.3, §1.5 (mean vs percentile on one sample; the dashboard remark — adjacent only)
  - 03-microbenchmarking-done-right.md §3.4.4 (minimum sample counts, distribution comparison — adjacent only)
  - 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.4.1 (CI tolerance and trend dashboard phrase — adjacent only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -nw -E 'SLO|SLOs|SLI|SLIs|SLA|SLAs' chapters/*.md = 0 lines (the case-insensitive hits are 'slow'/'slice'/'sliding'). grep -il: 'error budget', 'burn rate', pager, 'average of percentiles', 'histogram merge'/'merg.*histogram', hdr_add, t-digest/tdigest, DDSketch = 0 files; 'paging' hits are memory paging (Ch. 6/15). 'averag.*percentile' hits only Ch. 1 §1.3 point 1 and §1.5 — opened: they contrast the mean with percentiles on one sample and say 'if a dashboard reports average latency, ask at what percentile'; nothing on aggregating percentiles across hosts/windows. 'alert' hits (Ch. 44 §44.4.2, 51, 53, 58 §58.4.1/§58.5, 74 §74.4.3) are all 'alert on X' phrases with no threshold/window/hysteresis design. 'dashboard' only Ch. 1 §1.5 and Ch. 76 §76.4.1 ('a dashboard' for trend). Ch. 3 §3.4.4 gives benchmark-level rigor (sample counts ≫10^5 for p99.9, U-test comparison, multimodality) but not production aggregation; Ch. 76 §76.4.1 compares CI distributions against a baseline with a tolerance but defines no production objective. No section develops any of the three angles (percentile aggregation math, SLI/SLO framing, alert design).

## G05 — The control plane for a busy-poll process: command ring, health queries and operator actions without touching the hot thread

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#8
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area, but Ch. 73 already builds the core mechanism (shm segment + atomic version polled by the hot thread) for configuration; what remains is a compact, valuable generalisation rather than a missing capability.
- **Refined placement:** New §74.4.6 in Ch. 74 after §74.4.5, 'The control plane: commands and health queries without touching the hot thread', explicitly generalising Ch. 73 §73.4.3's config channel: a command slot/ring in the shared header polled once per loop iteration by the hot thread (one acquire load; measure it), a Unix-domain/TCP admin socket serviced by a blocking housekeeping thread (Ch. 41 §41.4.2, Ch. 50) that validates, authorizes (Ch. 72) and translates requests into shm commands with sequence-numbered acks; the command set (pause/resume per shard or symbol, flatten, set-limit routed through Ch. 73's validation, dump state/snapshot, arm/disarm — ties G06) and their semantics under the deterministic core (commands are inputs, captured for replay — Ch. 75); health queries answered from the telemetry segment (G01) so no query ever touches the hot thread; and a pitfall ('a signal handler or socket read on the hot thread is a syscall and a stall'). Write it alongside the G02 expansion since both extend the same shm header. No new chapter.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Mechanism largely exists for the config case; the delta is the general command vocabulary, the admin-socket design, ack/idempotency, authorization, and the measured per-iteration polling cost.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 73-hot-reload-live-reconfiguration.md §73.2, §73.4.1–§73.4.3 (admin/control process writes a shm config segment; atomic swap; validate-before-swap — the config instance of a control plane)
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.4.1 (one-bullet separation principle), §74.4.4 ('an operator command' as kill-switch trigger)
  - 50-inter-process-communication.md §50.2, §50.3, §50.5 (kernel IPC cost model; 'control-plane only')
  - 41-context-switching-mitigation.md §41.4.2 (control/admin threads block, hot thread busy-polls)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: 'command ring', 'command socket', 'command queue', 'command slot', 'admin socket', AF_UNIX, 'health endpoint', 'health check'/healthcheck, SIGUSR, 'pause symbol', 'signal handler', sigaction = 0 files. 'control plane'/'control-plane' hits opened: Ch. 74 §74.4.1 is one bullet ('Slow, rare control (config — Ch. 73, commands, kill-switch) can go over a separate channel'); Ch. 74 §74.4.4 names 'an operator command' as a kill-switch trigger in passing; Ch. 50 §50.2/§50.3/§50.5 establish that pipes/Unix-domain sockets (~120–250 ns, measured) are for control-plane IPC only; Ch. 41 §41.4.2 says control/admin threads should block; Ch. 38 §38.1 uses coroutines for control-plane connections; Ch. 71 §71.4.3 uses std::println for control-plane output. Ch. 73 §73.4.3 genuinely develops one control-plane command — config push via a shared-memory segment written by a separate control/admin process with an atomic version offset and validate-before-swap. 'flatten' hits are unrelated (loop flattening etc.). No command vocabulary (pause/resume per symbol, flatten, set-limit, dump state), no hot-thread command-slot polling cost, no admin-socket-to-shm translation, no request/ack semantics, no authorization of operator actions.

## G06 — The readiness gate and market-open playbook: startup-to-armed state machine, host attestation, fleet configuration as code and drift detection

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-appendix · **Sources:** health-app#9, health-os-net#4, sw-engineering#8, observability-depth#8, os-kernel-depth#9
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-os-net); the book refers to a readiness gate/check five times without defining it, and going live with an unsynced clock, a half-recovered book or an unreconciled position at the open is a correctness and compliance failure, not a latency one.
- **Refined placement:** Three-part, smallest footprint that does it justice: (1) new Appendix M 'Pre-Open Readiness Gate & Market-Open Playbook' after Appendix L, in Appendix C/I copy-paste style — a timed T-60→T-0 checklist with go/no-go criteria (host attestation: kernel cmdline, BIOS/microcode/NIC-firmware versions hashed against baseline, isolation integrity, hugepages/THP/swap/clocksource/mitigations, RDT masks, NIC ring/coalescing/offloads, PCIe link width/AER; clock gate: PTP offset and servo state; feed gate: A/B live, book rebuilt from snapshot, no open gaps; session gate: logon, sequence numbers reconciled; position/limits reconciled; warm-up complete), plus a host fingerprint micro-suite compared against Appendix E and the host's own history; (2) expand Ch. 74 §74.4.4 with the readiness state machine (cold → warming → syncing → reconciled → armed → trading → disarmed) published in the shm health header and gating the order path with one predicted branch (Ch. 13), driven by the control plane (G05); (3) new App C §C.6 'Configuration as code and drift detection' (declare Appendix C as an Ansible/Salt role or systemd unit tree versioned with the release; firmware/microcode inventory; periodic drift audit against the declared state; pointer to Appendix M). Fix Ch. 44's 'readiness check (Ch. 76)' and App C C.5's phrase to point at Appendix M. If a lighter footprint is required, drop (3)'s tooling to a paragraph inside C.6 and keep (1) and (2).
- **What is missing / scope notes:** What exists is a manual one-off verification list and repeated intent statements. Missing: the gate as an explicit, scripted, time-budgeted procedure with pass/fail criteria; the in-process armed state machine; re-arm after an intraday restart (continuous re-verification, not just pre-open); config-as-code, firmware inventory and drift detection across a fleet; the host fingerprint baseline. Keep the 'proving' flavour: every check is a command with an expected value, not prose.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.5 ('Verify the settings actually took', 'Re-verify after every change', the readiness-gate phrase), C.3 intro and preamble ('systemd unit, version-controlled')
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.3 (recovery stage table), §74.4.4 ('warm ... then go live')
  - 44-cache-allocation-intel-rdt.md §44.4.2 and §44.6 checklist (dangling 'readiness check (Ch. 76)')
  - 46-keeping-the-hot-path-warm.md §46.4 (warming as a go-live precondition)
  - 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md §58.4.1 (PTP offset bound)
  - 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.5 (drift named, no mechanism)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: 'readiness gate' = 1 file (App C C.5: 'ideally a pre-trading-day readiness gate'); 'readiness check' = 1 file (Ch. 44 §44.4.2 and its checklist: 'verify with CMT/MBM in the readiness check (Ch. 76)'), but grep readiness|health 76-*.md = 0 — dangling pointer confirmed. 'go live' = Ch. 74 §74.4.4 only ('then go live', no conditions). go/no-go, pre-open/preopen/pre-market, playbook, runbook, disarm, Ansible/Puppet/Salt, 'drift detection', dmidecode, 'golden image', 'configuration management', 'infrastructure as code', attestation, Redfish, ipmitool = 0 files; 'armed' hits are 'warmed'/'harmed'; 'microcode' hits (Ch. 27/28) are about instruction behavior; 'fingerprint' hits (Ch. 11–26) are 'the signature of' a stall. 'drift' opened: Ch. 76 §76.5 ('Drift between test and production', 'Tuning once and walking away'), Ch. 45 §45.5 ('Assuming tuned once = tuned forever ... re-run cyclictest after any change'), App C C.5 ('Verify the settings actually took' with a sysfs/proc list; 'Re-verify after every change ... bake into a periodic check'); App C intro and C.3 say 'version-control it'/'systemd unit' with no mechanism. Ch. 74 §74.3 has the detect→restart→recover→warm→live table and Ch. 46 the warm precondition; Ch. 58 §58.4.1 validates PTP offset continuously. The book invokes a readiness gate/check in five places and defines it nowhere; config-as-code and fleet drift detection have no content.

## G07 — Host health telemetry: proving the quiet box stayed quiet (isolation integrity, interrupts and IPIs, scheduler noise, THP/compaction, frequency/thermal throttling, SMI counts, clocksource demotion)

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#1, health-os-net#8, observability-depth#3, os-kernel-depth#4
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-os-net); the book's own pitfalls (Ch. 42 §42.5, Ch. 45 §45.5, App C C.5, Ch. 76 §76.5) say the quiet box decays and point to a continuous monitor in Ch. 76 that does not exist.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter 'Host Health Telemetry: Proving the Quiet Box Stayed Quiet', in Part XII immediately after the G01 telemetry chapter (so Part XII opens 71 Logging → telemetry → host health → network health → security …), renumbering with the existing script; alternative if the author prefers topic-locality: close Part VII after Ch. 46. Contents: isolation integrity as a live invariant (per-hot-thread voluntary/nonvoluntary ctxt switches, migrations and minor faults from /proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/status|schedstat|stat sampled from a housekeeping core; /proc/interrupts and /proc/softirqs deltas on isolated cores including the IPI rows; timer:tick_stop proving nohz_full engaged; tasks found on isolated cores; irqbalance/tuned/cpuset resurrected), frequency/thermal/power (thermal_throttle sysfs counts, RAPL/turbostat frequency residency, AVX license transitions) and SMI counting (MSR_SMI_COUNT 0x34, hwlat/rtla osnoise/timerlat for the hunt), memory-system health (THP/khugepaged/compaction vmstat counters, numastat remote growth, EDAC/MCE), clocksource demotion and the TSC watchdog, PSI, with the measurement of the monitor's own cost (MSR reads and /proc scans must not IPI or run on hot cores) and 'any nonzero delta is an incident' alert semantics feeding G01's segment and G06's gate. Fix the dangling pointers: Ch. 45 §45.4.1/§45.5 and App C C.1 'SMIs — Ch. 6', Ch. 45 §45.5 'monitor ... (Ch. 76)', Ch. 44 'readiness check (Ch. 76)'. Do not merge with G08 — each is a full chapter's worth; if the author insists on one, a single ~5k-word 'Host & Network Health' chapter with the forensic one-liners moved to Appendix I is the fallback.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** One-off verification recipes are scattered across Ch. 41/42/45/17 and App C; continuous monitoring exists only for RDT (Ch. 44) and PTP (Ch. 58). The chapter should consolidate the scattered checks into a sampled, cheap, continuous signal set with provenance (kernel, microcode, BIOS, NIC firmware) and a cost model for the sampler itself; keep rtla osnoise/timerlat as the 'hunt the residual spike' tool (currently absent) rather than re-teaching cyclictest.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 44-cache-allocation-intel-rdt.md §44.4.2 (continuous CMT/MBM monitoring with alerts — the one developed host-health monitor)
  - 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md §58.4.1, §58.5 (continuous PTP offset alerting)
  - 42-thread-interrupt-pinning.md §42.4.1 'Verify it stuck', §42.4.2 'Verify the core is quiet', §42.5 (one-off checks: Cpus_allowed, /proc/interrupts, perf stat cpu-migrations; irqbalance/stray-IPI pitfalls)
  - 41-context-switching-mitigation.md §41.3, §41.5, exercise 3 (zero switches/migrations target via perf stat)
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.3, §45.5 ('tuned once' pitfall pointing at Ch. 76), §45.6 exercise 5 (cyclictest -b/ftrace/proc/interrupts hunt)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.1 (SMI/RAPL BIOS knobs), C.5 (hwlat, /proc/interrupts, ps -eLo psr, 'verify the settings actually took')
  - 17-timekeeping-tsc-rdtsc-clocks.md §17.2.2, §17.3, §17.5 (one-off clocksource check)
  - 15-virtual-memory-tlb-huge-pages.md §15.4.1, §15.5, exercise 5 (khugepaged/compaction jitter as design choice)
  - 06-system-setup-for-low-latency.md §6.3 (cyclictest before/after), §6.4.1 (thermal throttling as a BIOS knob)
  - 29-simd-vectorization.md §29.3 (turbostat for AVX downclock)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: nonvoluntary_ctxt/voluntary_ctxt/ctxt_switches, /proc/softirqs, schedstat, sched_debug, thermal_throttle, MSR_SMI_COUNT, compact_stall, /proc/pressure/'pressure stall', hwlatdetect, osnoise, timerlat, rtla, trace-cmd, /proc/vmstat, getrusage, minflt/majflt, 'perf sched', mcelog, rasdaemon, 'patrol scrub', Bzy_MHz, aperf/mperf = 0 files. grep -nw SMI 06-*.md = 0 — so Ch. 45 §45.4.1/§45.5 and App C C.1 'SMIs — Ch. 6' are dangling; SMI content is only App C C.1 (BIOS knob) and C.5 (hwlat hunt). 'turbostat' = Ch. 29 §29.3/exercise (AVX downclock measurement) and Ch. 43 reference only. 'softirq' = Ch. 45 as RCU mechanism, not /proc/softirqs. 'khugepaged'/'compaction' = Ch. 15 §15.4.1/§15.5/exercise 5 and App C C.3 as a design-time choice. 'clocksource' = Ch. 17 §17.2.2/§17.3/§17.5 — verify current_clocksource is tsc once; runtime demotion only as an Additional Reading pointer. 'irqbalance' = Ch. 42 §42.4.2/§42.5 and Ch. 45 §45.5 ('config drift can silently re-enable irqbalance ... monitor the production tail continuously (Ch. 76)' — Ch. 76 has no host-health content). '/proc/interrupts' and 'cpu-migrations' opened: Ch. 42 §42.4.1 'Verify it stuck' and §42.4.2 'Verify the core is quiet', Ch. 41 §41.3/§41.5 ('perf stat ... zero involuntary switches and zero migrations during trading'), Ch. 45 exercise 5, App C C.5 (hwlat, /proc/interrupts, ps -eLo psr, verify settings) — all one-off verification recipes. Two genuinely continuous monitors exist: Ch. 44 §44.4.2 (CMT/MBM llc_occupancy/mbm_total_bytes polled in production with alerts) and Ch. 58 §58.4.1 (PTP offset). Nothing on continuous per-hot-thread counters from /proc/<tid>/status|schedstat, IPI rows (LOC/RES/CAL/TLB), nohz_full engagement proof, stray-task detection, thermal_throttle sysfs/RAPL/frequency residency, SMI count (MSR 0x34), TSC watchdog demotion, vmstat compaction counters, PSI, EDAC/MCE, or the cost of sampling (MSR reads IPI-ing isolated cores).

## G08 — Network health telemetry and drop forensics: NIC/driver/stack/socket/fabric counters, what each drop means, dropwatch and kfree_skb tracing, PFC and link health

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#2, networking-depth#1
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (health-monitoring-os-net); a silent drop at the open is a wrong book and a lost race, and the book instructs readers four times to 'monitor ethtool -S and alert' without ever explaining what the counters mean or where the packet died.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter 'Network Health Telemetry & Drop Forensics', in Part XII immediately after the G07 host-health chapter (the telemetry trio G01→G07→G08 following Ch. 71); alternative: close Part IX after Ch. 60 as the 'operate the NIC and fabric' chapter if the author wants it adjacent to Ch. 53/55/57. Contents: the counter hierarchy and what each layer's drop means — NIC MAC/PHY (ethtool -S rx_missed_errors ring-full vs rx_fifo_errors/rx_over PCIe backpressure — ties Ch. 66 — vs rx_no_buffer_count, per-queue drops, rx_crc/length/frame errors; driver dialects ixgbe/i40e/ice, mlx5 rx_out_of_buffer/rx_discards_phy, sfc), driver/NAPI (/proc/net/softnet_stat dropped and time_squeeze, netdev_budget), stack (nstat//proc/net/snmp IpInDiscards, UdpInErrors, UdpRcvbufErrors, TcpRetransSegs, TCPTimeouts, TCPSpuriousRTOs), socket (ss -tim / TCP_INFO rtt, retrans, notsent_bytes, busy_time; SO_MEMINFO), bypass stacks (DPDK rte_eth_stats imissed/rx_nombuf, ef_vi/Onload stats — fills Ch. 62 §62.5's gap), application (sequence gaps, A/B skew — G03); drop forensics (dropwatch, skb:kfree_skb tracepoint with drop reasons, perf net_dropmonitor, bpftrace one-liners for Ch. 61/App I); link and fabric health (pause frames, FEC corrected/uncorrected, symbol/CRC errors, link flaps, optics DOM, IGMP membership/querier state, switch drop counters); and the cost model and placement of the poller (ethtool ioctls run driver code — never on a hot core; sampling cadence vs alert latency). Forward-reference from Ch. 51 §51.5, Ch. 53 §53.4.4, Ch. 54 §54.3, Ch. 55 §55.5, Ch. 62 §62.5, Ch. 64 §64.4.3 and App I I.8, which today each carry a one-line 'monitor ethtool -S and alert'.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The book repeats the same three first-line counters in four places and names PFC monitoring as a requirement; everything beyond that — which layer dropped and why, the driver/stack/socket counters, bypass-stack counters, forensic tracing, and PHY/optics/fabric health — is absent. Pair the chapter with a measured market-open replay (Ch. 75) showing each counter class lighting up as the ring, the softirq budget and the socket buffer saturate in turn.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.2.4, §53.4.4 (three-layer drop detection with first-line counters)
  - 51-socket-optimization-tcp-tuning.md §51.4.2 'Detect drops', §51.5 (same list)
  - 55-nic-features-offloads.md §55.5, §55.6 checklist, exercises 3 and 5 (rx_missed/rx_no_buffer monitoring, per-queue ethtool -S)
  - appendix-I-tooling-cookbook.md I.8 (ethtool -S grep, ss -tim, interpretation note)
  - 64-lossless-ethernet-rdma-congestion.md §64.3, §64.4.3 (PFC PAUSE/ECN/retransmit monitoring requirement)
  - 60-network-fabric-switching.md §60.4.1 ('monitor error counters' phrase)
  - 62-kernel-bypass-userspace-networking.md §62.5 (lost kernel tooling under bypass)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -il: softnet_stat, time_squeeze, netdev_budget, rx_fifo, rx_over, rx_out_of_buffer, rx_nombuf, rx_crc, rx_discards, rx_queue_, InErrors, TCP_INFO/tcp_info/tcpi_, SO_MEMINFO, dropwatch, drop_monitor, kfree_skb, 'skb:', 'link flap', TcpRetrans, ksoftirqd = 0 files; nstat hit (Ch. 9) is 'constant' substring noise; 'FEC' hits are 'effect/affect' noise (no RS-FEC content); 'ss -ti'/'ss -tim' only App I I.8 (rtt, cwnd, retrans); 'IGMP' only App C C.3 one phrase. 'ethtool -S' = Ch. 51, 53, 55, App I — opened every one: Ch. 51 §51.4.2 'Detect drops' bullet and §51.5 (NIC ethtool -S rx_dropped/rx_missed, socket netstat//proc/net/udp, app sequence gaps); Ch. 53 §53.2.4 and §53.4.4 'Detect drops at every layer' (NIC rx_dropped/rx_missed/rx_no_buffer_count; UDP RcvbufErrors via /proc/net/snmp, ss -u; sequence gaps; 'instrument all three, alert on any'); Ch. 55 §55.5/§55.6 ('monitor ethtool -S rx_missed/rx_no_buffer'); App I I.8 (`ethtool -S | grep drop|miss|err|fifo`, `ss -tim`, interpretation 'non-zero RX drops mean the ring overflowed'). Ch. 64 §64.3 and §64.4.3 require measuring PFC PAUSE counts, ECN marking rate and RDMA retransmits continuously (Ch. 76) without naming the counters. Ch. 60 §60.4.1 'monitor error counters to confirm the links are clean' is one phrase; Ch. 62 §62.5 says bypass loses tcpdump/netstat and you must 'plan for observability in the bypass path' with no counters named. Ch. 61 has XDP_DROP and syscall/sched bpftrace one-liners but no skb:kfree_skb/drop_monitor tracing. No counter taxonomy by layer, no driver/NAPI or stack-level counters, no drop forensics, no link/PHY/optics health, no bypass-stack counters, no cost model for polling.

## G09 — Hunting residual noise on isolated cores: rtla osnoise/timerlat, hwlat, ftrace/trace-cmd/KernelShark, perf sched, SMI counting, and the kernel noise catalog (per-CPU kthreads, workqueues, RCU, TLB shootdowns, managed IRQs)

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#3, os-kernel-depth#2, os-kernel-depth#3
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Central to operating a tick-to-trade box (the residual p99.99 spikes are exactly what Ch. 45 leaves unattributed), explicitly in the author's health-monitoring-os-net area, and flagged independently by three analysts with zero coverage of the actual tools.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part VII, immediately after Ch. 45 and before Ch. 46 ('Hunting Residual Noise on Isolated Cores'), merging the two analysts' proposals into one: (a) the kernel noise catalog — per-CPU kthreads (ksoftirqd/migration/rcuc/watchdog/kworker), unbound-workqueue cpumask, isolcpus=domain,managed_irq,nohz and the housekeeping mask, rcu_nocbs/rcuo affinity, rcu_nocb_poll and expedited-GP IPIs, TLB-shootdown IPIs (munmap/THP/compaction/KSM), managed IRQs (NVMe), nmi_watchdog/perf NMIs, vmstat/kcompactd/khugepaged — each with its fingerprint and knob; (b) the attribution toolkit — rtla osnoise/timerlat (with stop-tracing thresholds), hwlat, tracefs anatomy (tracing_cpumask, trace_clock=x86-tsc, trace_marker, latency tracers, function_graph), trace-cmd/KernelShark, perf sched, SMI counting via MSR_SMI_COUNT/turbostat; §N.3 'Measure it' runs osnoise/timerlat beside Ch. 45's cyclictest table. Ch. 45 §45.3 and §45.6 ex. 5, Ch. 42 §42.5, Ch. 6 §6.4.1 and Appendix C.5 point at it; Appendix I gains an 'ftrace / rtla / perf sched' recipe section. Not a §45.4.3: the toolkit alone is chapter-sized and would double Ch. 45.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Existing coverage is the configuration side (how to make the core quiet) plus a single verification instrument; missing is the attribution side (how to find what is still landing on the core) and the catalog of residual sources with their knobs. Keep Ch. 45's cyclictest as the entry point and make the new chapter the 'if the max is still high' continuation. Overlaps: G13's NVMe managed-IRQ item and G16's spike-forensics chapter should cross-reference this chapter's tooling rather than re-teach it; G12's SMI/CMCI material shares the SMI-counting recipe.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.3 (cyclictest before/after table), §45.4.1 ('Verify the core is tickless/quiet': /proc/interrupts LOC), §45.5 (nohz_full >1 task; firmware/SMI pitfall), §45.6 ex. 5 (one line naming cyclictest -b + ftrace)
  - 42-thread-interrupt-pinning.md §42.4.2 (IRQ smp_affinity, move kworker/RCU off, 'verify the core is quiet'), §42.5 ('Stray IPIs and unmovable interrupts' named, not diagnosed)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.5 ('Hunt residual jitter sources': perf/ftrace/hwlat named as a one-liner)
  - 61-ebpf-bpftrace-xdp.md §61.4.1 (runqlat/offcputime/tracepoints as BCC tools)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'osnoise|timerlat|\brtla\b|hwlat|trace-cmd|kernelshark|function_graph|tracefs|/sys/kernel/(debug/)?tracing|ksoftirqd|workqueue|rcu_nocb_poll|managed_irq|shootdown' chapters/*.md -> rtla/osnoise/timerlat/trace-cmd/KernelShark/function_graph/tracefs/ksoftirqd/workqueue/rcu_nocb_poll/managed_irq = 0 hits; hwlat = 3 hits, all parenthetical (appendix-C.69/78, appendix-G.65); 'TLB shootdown' = 2 passing mentions (26-memory-mapping.md:147, 42-thread-interrupt-pinning.md:136). Synonym grep 'ftrace|SMI|perf sched|cyclictest -b|irqsoff|trace_pipe|trace_marker|kworker|IPI|MSR_SMI_COUNT|/proc/interrupts|softirq': ftrace named only in 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md:121 (exercise 5: 'use cyclictest -b + ftrace, and /proc/interrupts, to identify the source') and appendix-C.69; 'perf sched', MSR_SMI_COUNT, irqsoff, trace_pipe = 0. Read 45 §45.3-§45.6 (lines 50-125): the chapter is configuration (isolcpus/nohz_full/rcu_nocbs/irqaffinity/RT/throttling) verified only by cyclictest and /proc/interrupts LOC; the noise taxonomy is one line ('tick, RCU, kworkers, CFS preemption') and the §45.5 pitfalls name 'an un-offloaded kworker' and 'SMIs' without a fingerprint or a tool. 42 §42.4.2/§42.5 add IRQ smp_affinity and 'stray IPIs ... hard to fully eliminate' with no detection method. 61 §61.4.1 lists runqlat/offcputime but as BCC one-liners, not a jitter-attribution workflow.

## G10 — PTP and clock health: servo states, grandmaster clockClass/holdover, BMCA and redundant grandmasters, step-vs-slew policy when sync is lost, the clocksource watchdog

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#5, resilience-ha#10
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Valuable and in a requested area, but specialised and already framed by Ch. 58 ('desync is an outage, monitor the offset'); this deepens the 'how' rather than filling a hole in the tick-to-trade path.
- **Refined placement:** New §58.4.4 'Monitoring clock health and surviving a time-source failover' in Ch. 58 after §58.4.3: the ptp4l servo state machine (s0 unlocked / s1 stepped / s2 locked) as read from the -m log and pmc GET TIME_STATUS_NP (master_offset, gmPresent, gmIdentity); grandmaster health via PARENT_DATA_SET/GRANDMASTER_SETTINGS_NP and clockClass transitions (6 -> 7 holdover -> 52/187) as GPS is lost; BMCA with redundant grandmasters (priority1/priority2, domains, boundary-clock failover) and the offset discontinuity a re-election introduces, measured across an induced switch; host policy when the master disappears (ptp4l holdover/free-run, phc2sys step_threshold/first_step_threshold, slew vs step, flagging capture timestamps — ties Ch. 75); plus one §58.5 pitfall ('locked but wrong' / 'stepped mid-session') and one Ch. 17 §17.5 pitfall (the clocksource watchdog demoting tsc at runtime — dmesg 'Marking clocksource tsc as unstable', tsc=reliable/nowatchdog) with a matching Appendix C.5 verification item. A section, not a chapter.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The book already prescribes continuous offset monitoring and grandmaster holdover awareness; what is missing is the semantics of the signals it tells the reader to watch (servo state, clockClass), the fleet-level failover mechanism (BMCA/redundant GMs) and the host-side decision (slew vs step) when sync is lost, plus the runtime TSC-demotion hazard in Ch. 17. Both source proposals (health-os-net#5, resilience-ha#10) target the same new §58.4.4 and should be merged into it.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md §58.3 (ptp4l -m log line showing 's2'; pmc GET CURRENT_DATA_SET), §58.4.1 ('Monitor the sync continuously' bullet; PTP_SYS_OFFSET cross-timestamping), §58.4.3 ('Grandmaster oscillator drift and holdover' incl. 'monitor holdover'), §58.5 ('Not monitoring sync quality / undetected desync', 'Assuming sync is set-and-forget'), §58.6 ex. 4 and checklist items on monitoring/holdover
  - 17-timekeeping-tsc-rdtsc-clocks.md §17.2.2 (clocksource must be tsc; vDSO), §17.5 ('Wrong clocksource silently slow' — static check; 'CLOCK_REALTIME can step')
  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.5 (venue-side grandmaster/PPS/boundary clocks, orientation only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E '\bservo\b|clockClass|holdover|BMCA|best master|step_threshold|first_step|pmc |TIME_STATUS_NP|CURRENT_DATA_SET|PARENT_DATA_SET|clocksource watchdog|tsc unstable|unstable' chapters/*.md -> servo/BMCA/best master/step_threshold/TIME_STATUS_NP/PARENT_DATA_SET/clocksource watchdog/'tsc unstable' = 0 hits; 's2' appears only inside the ptp4l log line at 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md:60 and is never explained; holdover = 58:90/113/122/135/145 (grandmaster oscillator holdover, concept + 'monitor holdover'); pmc only as 'GET CURRENT_DATA_SET' at 58:61. Synonym grep 'phc2sys|ptp4l|grandmaster|slew|stepp|clock step|current_clocksource|leap second|free-run|priority1|domainNumber|redundant grand|gmPresent': ptp4l/phc2sys/grandmaster well covered in Ch. 58; stepping only as 'CLOCK_REALTIME can jump (NTP steps, leap seconds)' in 17:57/172; current_clocksource at 17:54/121/170/192 as a one-time static check; priority1/domainNumber/redundant grandmaster/gmPresent = 0. Read 58 §58.4.1-§58.5 (lines 86-128): 'Monitor the sync continuously' is one bullet (alert on offset threshold), §58.4.3 covers grandmaster holdover and oscillator grades, §58.5 says 'desync is an outage' and 'assuming sync is set-and-forget' — all policy, no servo-state semantics, no BMCA/re-election, no host step-vs-slew policy. Read 17 §17.2.2 and §17.5: 'Wrong clocksource silently slow' is a boot-time check; the kernel demoting tsc to hpet at runtime via the clocksource watchdog is not mentioned.

## G11 — Multicast group management and the colo multicast fabric: IGMP/MLD, snooping and the querier problem, join latency at the open, dark-feed detection, Linux traps (igmp_max_memberships, IP_MULTICAST_ALL, rp_filter)

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#6, networking-depth#3
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Every tick in the book arrives over multicast, the IGMP traps and the silent-feed failure are first-week production incidents for a new feed handler, and the topic has zero coverage in an explicitly requested area.
- **Refined placement:** New compact chapter in Part IX between Ch. 54 and Ch. 55 ('Multicast Group Management & Dark-Feed Detection'): Ch. 54 recovers the stream once it arrives; this chapter makes sure it arrives, on the right NIC, at the open, and that its absence is noticed. Scope: IGMPv2/v3 and MLD membership reports/queries/leaves; IGMP snooping and the querier problem (no querier -> entries age out -> feed silently stops after ~260 s); unknown-multicast flooding; PIM-SM/SSM and RPF in the exchange distribution tree vs L1 replication (Ch. 60); join latency and the market-open join storm (measured: join-to-first-packet distribution); SSM done right (IP_ADD_SOURCE_MEMBERSHIP/MCAST_JOIN_SOURCE_GROUP); Linux traps — igmp_max_memberships (default 20 -> ENOBUFS), IP_MULTICAST_ALL cross-talk, rp_filter dropping asymmetrically routed feeds, force_igmp_version, binding to the group vs INADDR_ANY; verifying membership from the host (/proc/net/igmp, ip maddr show, ip -s mroute, tcpdump igmp); the kernel-bypass case (Onload/ef_vi/DPDK joins); and the dark-feed watchdog (expected message-rate / exchange heartbeat / A-vs-B silence) wired to Ch. 54's stale-instrument and safe-state policy. Cross-refs: §51.4.2 forward pointer, a §54.5 pitfall ('a lost membership produces no gap'), Appendix C.3 item replacing 'tune igmp as needed', Appendix I.8 commands. Could shrink to a §54.4.4 if limited to membership health, but the join-latency + fabric + traps scope is chapter-sized and logically prior to gap recovery.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Nothing beyond the join call exists. The two source proposals disagree on footprint (section vs chapter); the chapter is justified because the material needs mechanism (IGMP/snooping), measurement (join latency, time-to-dark after querier loss) and code (membership verification, rate watchdog), and because Ch. 54 at 3,400 words would double. Keep the fabric-side PIM material brief and orientation-level, consistent with Ch. 60/Appendix K.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 51-socket-optimization-tcp-tuning.md §51.2 line 41 and §51.4.2 line 105 (UDP multicast as the transport; the join call, IP_MULTICAST_IF, SSM named in one sentence)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.3 line 47 ('tune igmp/reverse-path as needed')
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.4.4 and 54-reliable-multicast-feed-recovery.md §54.4.1/§54.5 (drop and gap detection only — not membership or silence)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E '\bIGMP|\bMLD\b|snooping|querier|igmp_max_memberships|IP_MULTICAST_ALL|/proc/net/igmp|ip maddr|\bPIM\b|rp_filter|join latency|reverse.path' chapters/*.md -> only appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md:47 ('tune igmp/reverse-path as needed' — a clause with no mechanism) plus one false positive ('stale copy' in Ch. 7). Synonym grep 'IP_ADD_MEMBERSHIP|IP_ADD_SOURCE_MEMBERSHIP|MCAST_JOIN|multicast group|membership|SSM|source-specific|IP_MULTICAST_IF|mroute|multicast flood|unknown multicast|dark feed|silent feed|allmulti': the join itself appears once, 51-socket-optimization-tcp-tuning.md:105 ('subscribers join the group (setsockopt IP_ADD_MEMBERSHIP, specifying the interface — IP_MULTICAST_IF/source-specific IP_ADD_SOURCE_MEMBERSHIP for SSM)'), plus 'multicast group' as a steering match key in Ch. 57/61; mroute/flood/dark feed = 0. Grep 'heartbeat|silen|no packets|stops arriving|stale|dead feed|no data' in 51/53/54: all 'heartbeat' hits are TCP connection warmth (Ch. 51 §51.4.1/Ch. 46); 'stale' hits are Ch. 54's per-instrument stale-after-gap policy. Read 51 §51.4.2 (lines 103-108), 54 §54.2/§54.5 (lines 19-45, 95-104), 53 §53.4.4 (lines 176-184): detection is sequence-gap and drop-counter based ('Detect drops at every layer'); the failure where membership lapses and the feed simply stops — no gap, no drop counter — is not addressed anywhere.

## G12 — Hardware health, RAS and device-link telemetry: PCIe AER and link retraining, ECC/CMCI/MCE, patrol scrub, firmware-first SMIs, EDAC/rasdaemon, errata

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#7, hardware-frontier#7
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Real but comparatively rare tail source and largely below the C++ layer; valuable in the requested area, yet less central than the kernel-noise, multicast, crash and forensics gaps.
- **Refined placement:** Split by footprint rather than a standalone chapter. (a) PCIe-link half: new §66.4.4 'Monitoring the link: negotiated width/speed, AER counters and retraining' in Ch. 66 after §66.4.3 — LnkCap vs LnkSta, /sys/bus/pci/devices/<bdf>/current_link_{speed,width} vs max_link_*, aer_dev_{correctable,nonfatal,fatal} counters and pcieport dmesg, what Receiver Error/Bad TLP/Replay Timer storms cost (link-level retries, Recovery-state retraining), causes of downtraining (riser/slot, firmware, ASPM) and pcie_aspm=off/pcie_port_pm=off; promote the §66.5 pitfall into this monitored signal and cross-ref from Ch. 55/69. (b) Host-RAS half (ECC SECDED/on-die, CMCI landing on an isolated core, MCE/MCA banks and mce= cmdline, patrol scrub bandwidth and interval, firmware-first vs OS-native (eMCA/GHES/APEI) and the SMIs it costs, EDAC ce_count, rasdaemon/ras-mc-ctl, SIGBUS on UCE, errata): a section of the OS/host-health monitoring chapter proposed in batch 1 (G07, Part XII) if that chapter is created; otherwise a new §6.4.4 'RAS modes and hardware-error telemetry' in Ch. 6 plus Appendix C.1 items (RAS mode, scrub interval, CMCI threshold) and a C.5 verification item. Appendix I.8/I.6 gain the lspci/ras-mc-ctl/mcelog one-liners.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Nothing exists beyond one pitfall and one BIOS checklist line. A dedicated RAS chapter is over-footprint for a C++ book: the reader needs the signals, the knobs and the latency cost (an SMI-handled corrected error on the hot core; a downtrained NIC raising the PCIe floor), not RAS engineering. The SMI-counting recipe should be shared with G09's noise-hunting chapter; the UCE/SIGBUS crash path ties to G15.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 66-pcie-host-device-boundary.md §66.5 line 129 (lspci -vv link width/speed pitfall) and §66.7 line 159 (lspci/lstopo/setpci reference)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.1 ('Disable SMIs where possible'; power-capping) and C.5 (hwlat detector named)
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.4.1/§45.5 (SMIs as a firmware jitter source to minimise)

## G13 — Capture-storage health and storage-path internals: NVMe SMART and I/O-latency outliers, writer backlog, dirty-page writeback stalls, blk-mq and NVMe managed IRQs, io_uring IOPOLL/SQPOLL, SPDK

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-os-net#9, os-kernel-depth#13
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Off-hot-path subsystem and specialised, but the capture journal is the compliance record and replay fixture, and the managed-IRQ/dirty-throttling mechanisms are real ways a 'quiet' box gets noisy; valuable in the requested area without being central to the tick-to-trade path.
- **Refined placement:** Expand Ch. 75 in place, no new chapter: (1) restructure §75.4.3 into grouped sub-bullets — writeback & durability (vm.dirty_background_ratio/bytes, dirty_expire/writeback_centisecs, per-bdi flusher kworkers, balance_dirty_pages throttling that can stall the writer itself; O_DIRECT vs RWF_DSYNC/fdatasync/sync_file_range for bounded durability windows; posix_fadvise(DONTNEED) for buffered fallbacks), blk-mq & NVMe (hardware queues per core, none vs mq-deadline for append-only, NVMe managed IRQs and keeping them off isolated cores — cross-ref G09), polled I/O (nvme poll_queues + IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL, when SPDK is the next tier); (2) add §75.4.6 'Keeping the capture path healthy' — ring high-water mark, writer-lag and drop counters published off-path like Ch. 76's histograms, nvme smart-log (percentage_used, media_errors, critical_warning, thermal-throttle counts), smartctl, biolatency/biosnoop I/O-latency outlier histograms, fill-level and end-of-life planning; (3) a §75.5 pitfall for NVMe managed IRQs landing on hot cores and dirty-page throttling stalling the writer; (4) Appendix C.3 items (dirty_* sysctls, nvme IRQ affinity, scheduler) and an Appendix I recipe line.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The 'what' (async writer, io_uring, O_DIRECT, NVMe, fsync granularity) and the hazard names exist; missing are the mechanisms behind the hazard (dirty-page throttling, blk-mq/managed IRQs) and any observability of the capture path (gauges, SMART, I/O-latency distribution). Both source proposals target Ch. 75 and should be merged; keep SPDK to a paragraph (it is a tier, not a chapter, for this book).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md §75.3 (ring-fill and writer-throughput measurement, figures pending), §75.4.3 (writer thread, io_uring, O_DIRECT, NVMe, fsync granularity), §75.5 ('Storage stall bleeding into the hot path', 'Dropping under load', 'Back-pressure reaching the hot path')
  - 48-io-uring-deep-dive.md §48.2 and §48.4.2 (IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL/SQPOLL, sq_thread_cpu pinning, polling CPU cost), §48.6 ex. 5 (O_DIRECT + IOPOLL async journaler)

  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.3 line 51 (writeback/dirty-page tuning bullet)

Read 75 §75.3 (lines 50-60), §75.4.3 (83-91), §75.5 (110-118): §75.3 says 'measure whether the ring ever fills ... the writer's sustained MB/s vs the burst's peak' (figures pending); §75.4.3 is five bullets (dedicated writer, io_uring, O_DIRECT, NVMe, fsync granularity 'without the writer falling behind'); §75.5 names 'storage stall bleeding into the hot path' and 'dropping under load' as hazards. ## G14 — Runtime resource gauges for zero-allocation systems: pool occupancy, allocation-after-warm-up alarms, page-fault and residency counters

- **Theme:** A. Health monitoring & telemetry · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** health-app#10
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium (was low)
- **Why this priority:** Falls in the author's explicitly requested health-monitoring-app area and guards a real market-open failure mode (pool exhaustion, allocation regression), but it is an incremental addition to an existing, well-developed discipline rather than a hole — so medium rather than the analyst's low or a blanket high.
- **Refined placement:** Expand in place, no new section numbers: (1) in Ch. 24 §24.4.2, grow the 'Capacity and exhaustion' bullet into a short 'Occupancy gauges and alarms' paragraph — current/high-water slots per pool and arena, ring occupancy, sampled by a housekeeping thread from counters the hot path already maintains (never by walking the pool), exported alongside the Ch. 76 histograms (or batch 1's telemetry segment if adopted), with a §24.5 pitfall ('a pool sized for yesterday's book depth') and a checklist line; (2) in Ch. 23 §23.5, one paragraph turning the test-time operator new counter into a per-thread relaxed production counter with an 'allocations after warm-up' alarm (Ch. 46 defines warm-up end), plus getrusage(RUSAGE_THREAD) ru_minflt/ru_majflt deltas per hot thread and /proc/self/smaps_rollup + HugePages_Free as residency checks; (3) Appendix I.7 one-liners for smaps_rollup/HugePages_Free; cross-ref Ch. 44's CMT monitoring as the established pattern.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The discipline (zero allocation, sized pools, explicit exhaustion policy) and its test-time proof are fully developed; only the production-telemetry angle is missing, and it is a few paragraphs. Keep the hot-path cost at a relaxed increment and make clear the sampling is off-path.

## G15 — Crash handling, core dumps and post-mortem diagnostics: async-signal-safe fault handlers, sigaltstack, signal cost/masking/funneling, flight recorders, minidumps and dumps that do not stall the box

- **Theme:** B. Debugging & diagnostics · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** debug-diag#1, os-kernel-depth#6
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Explicitly requested debugging-diagnostics area, central to operating a tick-to-trade system (a strategy faulting at the open with exposed orders is the canonical production incident), and the C++-on-Linux fault-handler mechanics are absent from the book.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part XII between Ch. 74 and Ch. 75 ('Crash Handling, Core Dumps & Post-Mortem Diagnostics'), absorbing and expanding §74.4.5 and forward-referenced from Ch. 5 §5.3/§5.4.2: async-signal-safe fault handlers (sigaction with SA_SIGINFO|SA_ONSTACK|SA_NODEFER, sigaltstack so stack overflow is still handled, handler restricted to write(2) of pre-formatted buffers — why glibc backtrace() is not AS-safe and must be pre-warmed or replaced by a frame-pointer/libunwind walk; reading si_addr/si_code/ucontext registers; SIGSEGV vs SIGBUS (hugetlbfs, MAP_SHARED truncation) vs SIGFPE/SIGILL; std::terminate/abort paths); the kill-switch handoff from the dying process to the gateway (ties §74.4.4) and dumping the flight-recorder ring (ties G16); dumps that do not stall the box — coredump_filter and madvise(MADV_DONTDUMP) to exclude the hugepage-backed book/rings, RLIMIT_CORE, core_pattern pipe handler design (bounded runtime, housekeeping cores, nice/ionice, non-capture disk), systemd-coredump interaction, minidumps (Breakpad/Crashpad) and when a full core is still worth it; §N.3 measures signal round-trip cost, dump duration vs size with/without filter, and the survivors' tail during a dump. Plus a short new §41.4.3 in Ch. 41 ('Signals: delivery cost, masking and funneling') — sigframe/XSAVE cost, which thread a process-directed signal lands on, pthread_sigmask on hot threads at start, funneling via signalfd/sigwaitinfo on a housekeeping thread, SA_RESTART/EINTR — since signal delivery is a context-switch-class event. Appendix I gains the core_pattern/coredumpctl/gdb one-liners.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Core dumps are covered as policy and post-mortem method; the crash-time machinery (handler code, dump-size control, pipe handler, minidumps, kill-switch sequencing) and signals as a hot-path cost have zero coverage. A chapter is justified because Ch. 74 is already Part XII's longest chapter (4,400 words) and the material needs code and measurement; the two source proposals split cleanly into the chapter (crash path) and the §41.4.3 section (signal cost/masking).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.3 table (detect via SIGCHLD/heartbeat), §74.4.3 (supervisor, heartbeats catch hangs), §74.4.4 (kill-switch: gateway cancels the dead strategy's orders; crash-only restart with warm-up), §74.4.5 (one bullet on core dumps without stalling survivors: separate disk, bound/compress, core_pattern pipe, minimal dump), §74.5 pitfall
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.3 ('Core dumps for the crash you can't reproduce'), §5.4.1 (archive debug info, -gsplit-dwarf/objcopy), §5.4.2 ('Core-dump post-mortem' in gdb; minidumps mentioned), §5.5 ('Core dumps stalling survivors'), §5.7 (core(5)/minidump tooling reference)
  - 20-the-cost-of-abstractions.md §20.2/§20.5 (std::terminate on noexcept violation; -fno-exceptions -> abort) — terminate path only
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'sigaction|SA_SIGINFO|SA_ONSTACK|sigaltstack|async-signal|signal-safe|core_pattern|coredump_filter|minidump|breakpad|crashpad|pthread_sigmask|sigprocmask|signalfd|sigwait|MADV_DONTDUMP|SIGSEGV|SIGBUS|SIGABRT|SIGQUIT|SIGUSR|SIGCHLD|signal handler|siginfo|core.?dump|backtrace|post-?mortem|flight.?record|RLIMIT_CORE|gcore|systemd-coredump|libunwind|std::stacktrace|set_terminate' chapters/*.md -> sigaction/SA_SIGINFO/SA_ONSTACK/sigaltstack/async-signal-safe/coredump_filter/Breakpad/Crashpad/pthread_sigmask/signalfd/MADV_DONTDUMP/SIGSEGV/SIGUSR/'signal handler'/siginfo/RLIMIT_CORE/libunwind/std::stacktrace = 0 hits; SIGBUS once as a split-lock aside (09-object-layout:212); SIGCHLD only as the supervisor's exit-detection in 74-process-topology:81/119/147/159; core_pattern and minidump each appear once as a word (74:135 'use core_pattern piping to an async handler that doesn't block, or capture a minimal dump'; 05:55 'Minidumps / partial cores (Ch. 74)'). Read 74 §74.4.3-§74.4.5 (lines 115-137): the core-dump treatment is one bullet (separate disk, bound/compress, core_pattern pipe, minimal dump); the kill-switch is executed by the surviving gateway via cancel-on-disconnect. Read 05 §5.3/§5.4.1/§5.4.2/§5.5 (31-60, 72-78): core-dump post-mortem methodology (archive -g/-gsplit-dwarf, gdb bt, pair with capture replay) and the 'core dumps stalling survivors' pitfall — policy and analysis, no handler code, no dump-size control, no signal cost or masking anywhere in the book.

## G16 — Latency-spike forensics and anomaly-triggered diagnostics: attributing a one-off tail event (timeline alignment across clocks, Intel PT, perf snapshot mode, osnoise), always-on flight-recorder rings frozen on trigger, and the incident diagnostics bundle

- **Theme:** B. Debugging & diagnostics · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** debug-diag#2, observability-depth#11, debug-diag#7
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Explicitly requested debugging-diagnostics area and central to operating a tick-to-trade system — the one-off 40 µs stall that costs the fill is the event the whole book optimises against, and today the book can only say that it happened, not why.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part XII between Ch. 75 and Ch. 76 ('Latency-Spike Forensics & Anomaly-Triggered Diagnostics') so Ch. 76 remains the capstone: (1) the forensic method — from one p99.99 outlier in the per-stage histograms to a mechanism: reconstruct a single timeline across clocks (TSC -> CLOCK_MONOTONIC -> PHC/PTP -> ftrace trace_clock -> dmesg/CLOCK_BOOTTIME via PTP_SYS_OFFSET_PRECISE, Ch. 17/58), pin core/thread/window, test each suspect against its fingerprint (operationalising Ch. 1 §1.4.3's table: /proc/interrupts and /proc/softirqs deltas, irq:* tracepoints, tlb:tlb_flush and IPIs, THP/compaction, RT throttle, SMI counter deltas, C-state exits, CMT/MBM neighbour spikes, PTP steps, venue-side causes) — consuming G09's rtla/ftrace tooling by cross-reference rather than re-teaching it; (2) always-on capture frozen on trigger — perf record in overwrite/snapshot mode (--overwrite/--snapshot with SIGUSR2 or fifo control), Intel PT (perf record -e intel_pt// snapshot, PTWRITE, decode cost), per-thread flight-recorder rings of binary trace records (Ch. 71's policy) frozen by swapping the ring pointer so the hot path keeps writing while a housekeeping core drains the frozen one; triggers (latency over budget, stall detector, gap/reject burst, PTP desync, CMT spike); measured: trigger-to-freeze latency and the hot-path cost of the always-on ring; (3) the incident bundle as the chapter's checklist — build-id/readelf -n and -frecord-gcc-switches, config hash (Ch. 73), dmesg/journalctl window, /proc/interrupts and PSI snapshots, PTP/servo state, NIC drop counters, perf archive — plus an Appendix I.10 'incident collection' recipe section rather than a new appendix (if batch 1's pre-open readiness-gate appendix is created, fold both into one operations-runbook appendix). Ch. 45 §45.6 ex. 5, Ch. 1 §1.4.3 and Appendix C.5 point at it; Ch. 5 §5.4.3 forward-references it as the 'you weren't tracing the right thing at 09:31:42' answer.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The book has the observation primitives and the replay-based incident path, but no method that starts from a single tail event and ends at its mechanism, no trigger-frozen capture, and no hardware/kernel trace in snapshot mode. Three source proposals (debug-diag#2, observability-depth#11, debug-diag#7) converge; the chapter should own the method and the flight recorder, G15's crash chapter should own the crash-time dump, and G09 should own kernel-noise attribution tooling — cross-reference, don't duplicate.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 01-the-latency-mindset.md §1.4.3 ('Identifying and attacking sources of jitter' table: signatures and chapters)
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.3 (capture-driven reproduction of a one-off), §5.4.3 ('Production tracing without perturbing the hot path': eBPF, lock-free log as debug channel, perf counters), §5.5 (observer effect)
  - 61-ebpf-bpftrace-xdp.md §61.4.1 (bpftrace rare-condition filters; runqlat/offcputime/biolatency; USDT markers)
  - 71-zero-overhead-logging.md §71.6 ('overwrite oldest' full-buffer policy — the flight-recorder ring shape, as a logging policy only)
  - 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md §58.4.1/§58.5 (PTP_SYS_OFFSET_PRECISE cross-timestamping TSC<->PHC)
  - 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.2 (continuous monitoring: per-stage HdrHistograms, counters, eBPF, capture; deep analysis offline on replay), §76.4.2 ('Debug production incidents by replay')
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'intel pt|intel_pt|processor trace|--snapshot|snapshot mode|flight.?record|on (an )?outlier|PTP_SYS_OFFSET|tlb_flush|incident|sosreport|perf archive|build-?id|dmesg|journalctl|forensic|one-off|timeline|magic-trace|\bLBR\b|perf sched|runqlat|offcputime|/proc/softirqs|frozen' chapters/*.md -> Intel PT/intel_pt/processor trace/--snapshot/flight recorder/trigger-on-outlier/tlb_flush/sosreport/perf archive/build-id/dmesg/journalctl/forensic/magic-trace/perf sched//proc/softirqs = 0 hits; PTP_SYS_OFFSET_PRECISE at 58-clock-synchronization-ptp.md:95/126/147 (TSC-to-PHC cross-timestamping — one axis of timeline alignment); 'one-off' at 05-debugging:33 ('turning a one-off into something you can study' — via capture-replay); 'incident' at 76-production-profiling:82/123 ('debug production incidents by replay' of the 09:31:42 window) and appendix-I:142 (check ethtool drops after a market-open incident); runqlat/offcputime at 61-ebpf:87/93 as BCC tools; LBR only as perf call-graph/BOLT input; 'frozen' only as Ch. 73 config versions. Read 01 §1.4.3 (jitter-source table: signature + chapter — a map, not a workflow), 05 §5.3/§5.4.3 (eBPF probes, lock-free log as debug channel, perf counters; 'gather in production, reproduce offline' — no trigger, no timeline reconstruction), 61 §61.4.1 (bpftrace '/lat > 100000/' rare-condition filter), 71 §71.6 ('overwrite oldest' as a logging full-buffer policy, never frozen on a trigger), 76 §76.2 (per-stage histograms, counters, eBPF, capture; deep analysis offline on replay) and §76.4.2 (incident debugging by replay). ## G17 — Live introspection without stopping the hot thread, and memory-corruption forensics without sanitizers: out-of-process reads of versioned state, gdb non-stop mode and Python pretty-printers for lock-free structures, hardware data breakpoints via perf_event_open, guard pages, poison, hardened-library modes

- **Theme:** B. Debugging & diagnostics · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** debug-diag#3, debug-diag#8
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high (was medium)
- **Why this priority:** Author-requested area (debugging-diagnostics) and an operating-central hole: the book removes gdb, printf and the sanitizers from production (Ch. 5, 40, 72) without giving the engineer any way to inspect, or do corruption forensics on, a live trading process.
- **Refined placement:** Keep the analysts' placement; no new chapter. Ch. 5 gains two technique subsections: §5.4.4 'Looking inside a live process without stopping the hot thread' (all-stop vs non-stop attach with the attach/detach stall measured on the hot thread's p99.9 and its propagation to shm consumers per Ch. 50; `gdbserver --attach` + `set non-stop on`/`scheduler-locking` to inspect a housekeeping thread; out-of-process reads of versioned/seqlock state via `process_vm_readv`//proc/PID/mem against the Ch. 35/50 segments; gdb Python pretty-printers for the Ch. 34/37 rings and the Ch. 25 book; Yama `ptrace_scope`) and §5.4.5 'Finding the corrupting write in a zero-allocation design' (`perf_event_open(PERF_TYPE_BREAKPOINT, HW_BREAKPOINT_W)` / `perf record -e mem:ADDR:w -p PID` on a live PID, the four-debug-register limit; guard pages/`mprotect` around pool slabs with their page/TLB cost; poison/clear-on-free; `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` / `_LIBCPP_HARDENING_MODE` as affordable production modes). Rewrite the §5.5 pitfall from 'never attach' to 'never all-stop attach'. Add hardened-library rows to Ch. 72 §72.3's table and Appendix D.7; cross-reference from Ch. 24 §24.5 and Ch. 40 §40.5.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The live-introspection half is wholly absent — every existing mention is the prohibition. The forensics half should reuse, not duplicate, §72.4.2's generation-counter/clear-on-free techniques and §5.4.2's watchpoint idea; what is new is doing it on a live PID (debug registers via perf_event_open, per-thread inheritance, sidecar process), guard pages as a deliberate pool-slab technique (tie Ch. 15/23 for the fault/TLB cost), and the hardened-library modes measured on the decode and book-update loops so they can take rows in the §72.3 table. Include the measurement block: ptrace attach/detach stall and a breakpoint-armed-but-not-hit run against the hot thread's p99.9.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.4.2 (hardware watchpoints and conditional breakpoints — replay/rr only, 'never the live hot path')
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.4.3 (production observation limited to bpftrace / binary log / PMU counters)
  - 05-debugging-low-latency-optimized-code.md §5.5 ('Attaching gdb to the live hot path' — the prohibition, no safe alternative)
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.4.2 (arena lifetime discipline: generation counters, clear-on-free in debug, ASan in test)
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.3 (hardening-vs-latency table — no hardened-library-mode row)
  - 40-concurrency-correctness-tooling.md §40.5 ('Running sanitizers in production' pitfall)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Ran: grep -n -i -E 'non-stop|nonstop|scheduler-locking|pretty.?printer|gdb python|python api|process_vm_readv|ptrace_scope|yama|PERF_TYPE_BREAKPOINT|HW_BREAKPOINT|mem:0x|guard page|_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS|_LIBCPP_HARDENING|hardened|gdbserver|gdb -p|\bptrace\b|debug register|watchpoint|poison|redzone|canary|PROT_NONE|mprotect|MALLOC_PERTURB' /home/user/ClaudeTeaches/chapters/*.md.
