## G64 — Schema evolution of shared-memory layouts and IPC messages across independently deployed processes: version headers vs per-message versions, compatibility windows, layout fingerprints, rolling upgrades

- **Theme:** G. Engineering lifecycle · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** sw-engineering#6
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Valuable but specialised: Ch. 50's fail-loudly version check is a valid lockstep-upgrade policy today, and the evolution mechanics matter chiefly as the prerequisite for G63's rolling cutover and Ch. 74's promise of independently restartable roles.
- **Refined placement:** Section-level, as proposed: grow the two §50.4.2 bullets into a new §50.4.3 'Schema evolution: versioning, compatibility windows and rolling upgrades' in Ch. 50 (the chapter that already states the principle and whose checklist already demands a versioned header). Add a short §53.2.5 (or a paragraph in §53.2.1) on the SBE message header — schemaId, version, templateId, blockLength — and its extension rules (append-only fields, blockLength-driven skipping, acting-version checks) as the industry-standard embodiment of the same idea. Cross-reference Ch. 73 §73.4.2 (config version), Ch. 74 §74.2.1 (independently restarted roles), Ch. 9 §9.3 (static_assert tables) and the proposed release-engineering chapter (G63), whose standby cutover depends on this.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Already covered: why layouts are a cross-process contract, the magic/version header checked on attach with fail-loud mismatch, fixed-width/no-implicit-padding structs (Ch. 9/50), versioned capture and config. Missing: the evolution mechanics — segment-level version vs per-message type/version byte vs per-field presence; the compatibility window (which (reader, writer) version pairs must interoperate, enforced at attach); additive-only field evolution with reserved padding and blockLength-style skipping so an old reader ignores new trailing fields and a new reader tolerates short old records; why layouts drift even without source changes (compiler/flag/ABI differences between separately built processes, re-padding for Ch. 33 alignment); layout fingerprints (hash of pahole/DWARF output or a C++26 reflection-derived digest) compared at attach and embedded in the capture header (Ch. 75); the rolling-upgrade sequence for process-per-role (upgrade readers before writers or vice versa, per the change) and a test that runs mixed versions against a shared ring; the hot-path cost of a per-message version check (one predictable branch — verify in asm, Ch. 4) vs a once-at-attach check. Keep it short; the SBE paragraph should reference the spec rather than re-teach it.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 50-inter-process-communication.md §50.4.1 (line 137): slot layout is a wire format — 'version it'; §50.4.2 (lines 146–147): fixed-layout struct, version/magic header checked on attach, fail loudly, 'treat layout changes like protocol changes'; §50.5 (lines 156, 160): unversioned-layout and stale-segment pitfalls; exercise 5 (line 171); checklist (line 178)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.2.1 (lines 24–31): SBE as schema-defined fixed binary, overlay accessors — no header/versioning semantics; §53.2.2: overlay discipline and bounds checks
  - 73-hot-reload-live-reconfiguration.md §73.4.2 (line 77): monotonic config version, recorded per decision for replay
  - 75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md §75.4.2 (line 79), §75.5 (line 119), checklist (line 139): versioned, self-describing journal format archived with its decoder
  - 09-object-layout-alignment-padding.md §9.3 (lines 101–105, 123): static_assert(sizeof/alignof/offsetof) and pahole as the layout-pinning tools; 33 §33.5 (line 147): padding changes break serialization
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps across chapters/*.md: 'schema (evolution|version|id)|schemaId|layout version|rolling (upgrade|restart|deploy)|mixed.version|version skew|forward.compat|backward.compat' → 0; 'blockLength|templateId|SBE.*(header|version|extension)' → 0; 'fingerprint|pahole.*(hash|dump)|DWARF.*(hash|dump)' → 0; '\bmagic\b' and 'version' in Ch. 50 → §50.4.1 line 137 ('The slot layout is a wire format across processes — version it (Ch. 53)'), §50.4.2 lines 146–147 ('Define a fixed, versioned layout… put a version/magic field in a header, and check it on attach so a version mismatch fails loudly… Treat layout changes like protocol changes'; header with magic/version, ring offsets, PIDs), §50.5 lines 156, 160 (unversioned-layout pitfall; stale-segment detection via header version/PID), exercise 5 line 171, checklist line 178, references line 194 (Aeron/Chronicle 'versioned layouts'). Opened Ch. 53 §53.2.1 lines 24–31: SBE described as 'fixed-layout binary defined by a schema… overlay generated accessors' — no schemaId/version/blockLength/extension semantics; §53.2.2 overlay + bounds-check discipline. Ch. 73 §73.4.2 (line 77): monotonically increasing config version recorded with decisions; Ch. 75 §75.4.2 (line 79) and §75.5 (line 119): capture format 'self-describing and versioned', archive the decoder; Ch. 9 §9.3: static_assert pins and pahole for layout. Ch. 33 §33.5 line 147: padding can break serialization. So the fail-loudly version-check principle is stated clearly in Ch. 50, but nothing teaches evolving a layout while old and new processes coexist.

## G65 — Resilience verification: a fault catalogue for the trading stack, deterministic fault injection as sequenced inputs, game days, failover drills with measured recovery, latency-incident postmortems

- **Theme:** G. Engineering lifecycle · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** sw-engineering#9, resilience-ha#8
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Valuable operating discipline that makes Ch. 74's containment and Ch. 54's recovery measurable before the open does it, but it is methodology rather than C++/Linux mechanism, is not in an author-requested area, and both source analysts rated it medium.
- **Refined placement:** Not a new chapter unless an HA mini-part materialises from G66/G67. Smallest footprint that does it justice: a new §76.4.4 'Resilience gates: fault injection, failover drills and latency-incident postmortems' in Ch. 76 (which already owns the CI gates and the simulation harness it extends), containing the fault catalogue as a table that names the owning chapter per fault, the 'faults as sequenced inputs to the deterministic core' mechanism, the network-level injection recipes (tc netem, iptables drop of the venue session, ip link down, PTP loss), and a measured detect-to-live drill protocol; plus a one-paragraph hook in Ch. 74 §74.3 pointing at it. Appendix I gains the netem/iptables one-liners.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Missing: a fault catalogue with expected blast radius per fault; deterministic fault injection as sequenced inputs so a failover scenario replays bit-identically and can be bisected; network/OS-level injection tooling (tc netem delay/loss/partition, iptables, link flap, clock step, disk-full, OOM); scheduled game days / failover drills with measured recovery distributions (not single runs); latency-incident postmortem practice tied to the Ch. 76 per-stage histograms and Ch. 75 capture. Existing: the recovery metric (Ch. 74 §74.3), the simulator bullet (Ch. 76 §76.4.3), and ad hoc exercise-level injection.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - chapters/76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.4.3 (one bullet 'Inject the rare and the hostile' + checklist line 124) and §76.4.2 ('Debug production incidents by replay')
  - chapters/74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.3 (detect/restart/recover/warm/live recovery metric and its tail) and §74.6 exercises 1, 3, 5 (ad hoc segfault/kill/hang injection)
  - chapters/40-concurrency-correctness-tooling.md §40.4.1 (stress/soak/load-replay, concurrency-only)
  - chapters/54-reliable-multicast-feed-recovery.md §54.4.3 (trading-through-a-gap policy, safe-state escalation)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps run across chapters/*.md (case-insensitive): 'fault.injection|fault-inject|inject(ed|ing)? (a |the )?(fault|failure|crash|segfault|gap)' -> only Ch. 53 ex. 3 ('Inject a gap in both'), Ch. 74 ex. 1 ('Inject a segfault'), Ch. 76 §76.4.3 one bullet ('Inject the rare and the hostile: microburst, gap/recovery, malformed packet, failover') + its checklist echo; 'chaos|game.?day|fire.?drill|failover drill' -> 0; '\bdrill\b' -> only 'drill down' (Ch. 2, App. I); 'netem|iptables|link flap|ip link set' -> 0 ('tc qdisc' appears only in Ch. 59 for the ETF qdisc); 'postmortem|post-mortem|blameless|incident review|runbook|playbook|MTTR' -> 'post-mortem' only in Ch. 5 §5.2/§5.4.2 in the core-dump/debugger sense, 'incident' only Ch. 76 §76.4.2 'Debug production incidents by replay' (one bullet). Opened Ch. 74 §74.3 (recovery metric table detect->restart->recover->warm->live; a measurement framework, not a drill method), Ch. 74 §74.6 (exercises 1/3/5 kill/hang a process ad hoc), Ch. 76 §76.4.1-76.4.3 (CI gates and simulation harness; fault injection is one bullet with no catalogue, no deterministic fault-as-input mechanism, no tooling), Ch. 40 §40.4.1 (stress/soak is concurrency-interleaving stress, not system fault injection), Ch. 54 §54.4.3 (gap policy, not drills). The idea exists; the method, catalogue, network-level injection, drills and postmortem practice do not.

## G66 — Failover without split-brain on the order path: cross-host failure detection over two paths, leases and fencing tokens, exactly-one-emitter per venue session, exchange-side safety nets (cancel-on-disconnect, mass cancel), measured detect-to-promote time

- **Theme:** H. Resilience & high availability · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** resilience-ha#2
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** A failover that lets two gateways send under one session is worse than the outage Ch. 74 exists to contain, the book's 'keep the venue session up' promise is unfulfilled without it, and nothing beyond two passing mentions of cancel-on-disconnect exists.
- **Refined placement:** A new chapter is justified: Part XII, immediately after Ch. 74 (Process Topology) and before Ch. 75, titled along the lines of 'Failover Without Split-Brain: Leases, Fencing & Session Takeover on the Order Path'. If the orchestrator also carries the order-entry-session-resilience proposal (resilience-ha#3), merge the two into this one chapter (session sequence numbers/reconnect are the mechanism the takeover uses). Do not fold it into Ch. 74: Ch. 74 is already the longest chapter in Part XII (4,400 words) and is single-host by design. Fix the dangling Ch. 65 §65.8 'consensus material (Ch. 74)' pointer to the new chapter.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Must cover: split-brain as a financial invariant (two emitters under one venue session = doubled position + regulatory event); cross-host failure detection over two independent paths (in-host shm heartbeat from Ch. 74 plus a cross-host side channel), fixed timeouts vs phi-accrual and the detect-time/false-positive trade; leases on monotonic clocks (Ch. 17) and fencing tokens checked by the gateway before every send; the exactly-one-emitter rule and where it is enforced (gateway, session, exchange); exchange-side safety nets (cancel-on-disconnect, mass cancel, drop-copy reconciliation) defined properly; a measured detect-to-promote distribution. Reuse Ch. 74 §74.3's recovery-metric framing and Ch. 65's Aeron Cluster as the off-the-shelf alternative.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - chapters/74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.4.3 (single-host supervisor, heartbeats, restart policy) and §74.4.4 (kill-switch in the surviving gateway; 'cancel-on-disconnect' named in passing)
  - chapters/65-transport-beyond-tcp.md §65.4.1 (one bullet on Aeron Cluster / Raft replicated state machines, pointing to a Ch. 74 that has no such material)
  - chapters/appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.6 (one bullet on redundant gateways as a failover path)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps run across chapters/*.md (case-insensitive): 'split.brain' -> 0; 'leader.election|leader' -> 0; 'fencing token|fence token' -> 0 ('fencing' hits are memory fences in Ch. 10/17/30/63); '\blease\b' -> only Ch. 44 §44.2 metaphor ('a CLOS is a lease on cache ways'); 'quorum|raft|paxos|consensus' -> only Ch. 65 §65.4.1 one bullet ('Aeron Cluster provides Raft-based ... replicated state machines for consensus/failover') and Ch. 65 §65.8 'the Aeron Cluster / consensus material (Ch. 74)' -- a dangling pointer, Ch. 74 has no consensus content; 'cancel.on.disconnect' -> Ch. 74 §74.2.1 line 37 and §74.4.4 line 127, both in passing ('can cancel-on-disconnect / mass-cancel'), never defined or designed around; 'exactly.one (sender|emitter)' -> 0 ('exactly one writer' hits are the single-writer memory discipline in Ch. 26/34/37/50); 'phi.accrual|accrual|STONITH' -> 0; 'failover|standby|takeover|promot(e|ion)|network partition|cross.host|second host' -> Ch. 74 §74.3 title 'failover/restart time' (content is single-host restart), Appendix K.6 one bullet ('Redundant cross-connects and gateways give order entry a failover path; sessions reconnect and resynchronize (J.3)' -- and J.3, opened, covers the order state machine only, not session resync), Appendix K.3 microwave-to-fiber failover. Opened Ch. 74 §74.4.3-74.4.4: supervisor/heartbeats are in-host via shared-memory slots and SIGCHLD; the kill-switch lives in the surviving gateway. Nothing on cross-host failure detection, leases, fencing, exactly-one-emitter enforcement, exchange-side nets, or measured detect-to-promote.

## G67 — State checkpointing and snapshot/restore for fast restart (fork/COW snapshots and their stall cost, double-buffered state, snapshot at a sequence boundary, RTO curve) and shared-memory crash consistency: segment epochs, torn-write detection, the reattach protocol, robust mutexes

- **Theme:** H. Resilience & high availability · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** resilience-ha#5, resilience-ha#6
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Ch. 74's crash-only fast restart -- the book's stated containment strategy -- is unimplementable without these mechanics, and two earlier chapters already promise the reader this content lives in Ch. 74.
- **Refined placement:** Expand Ch. 74 with two new technique sections, exactly where Ch. 26 and Ch. 50 already point: §74.4.6 'Checkpointing the core: consistent snapshots without stalling the hot path' (snapshot as a (state, last-applied-seq) pair at a sequence boundary; fork()/COW snapshot and its measured parent-side cost -- COW fault storm, TLB shootdowns, huge-page interaction, Ch. 15/23; double-buffered state as the alternative; the RTO curve = detect + load(size) + replay(rate x interval) + warm, added as a measured row to the §74.3 table) and §74.4.7 'Segment lifecycle: reattach, epochs and torn-write recovery' (header discipline: magic, layout version, epoch/generation, owner PID + start time or pidfd, heartbeat slot, checksum; the reattach decision tree; commit-flag / sequence-last ordering from Ch. 35 so a half-written slot is detectable; writer-epoch stamping so readers discard a dead epoch's tail; robust mutexes / EOWNERDEAD for the rare cross-process lock). Ch. 75 §75.4.4 keeps only the storage/indexing side and points at §74.4.6.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Existing: the need for snapshots and the recovery-time decomposition (Ch. 74 §74.3, Ch. 75 §75.4.4); a header sketch with magic/version/PIDs (Ch. 50 §50.4.2); seqlock torn-read avoidance and double-buffering in-process (Ch. 35 §35.4.2). Missing: any snapshot-taking mechanism and its measured hot-path cost, the RTO curve, the reattach decision tree and epoch/torn-record handling for shared-memory segments after a writer dies mid-store, and robust mutex semantics. The two 'Ch. 74' pointers in Ch. 26 and Ch. 50 currently lead nowhere.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - chapters/74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.3 (recovery table: 'bounded by snapshot frequency'; lesson 'Bound recovery state with snapshots') and §74.4.4 (replay from last snapshot, crash-only restart)
  - chapters/75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md §75.4.4 ('Time-indexing and snapshots' bullet) and §75.5 ('No snapshots -> unbounded replay')
  - chapters/50-inter-process-communication.md §50.4.2 (segment header with magic/version/PIDs, version check on attach) and §50.5 (orphaned segment / producer died mid-write -- problem only, defers to Ch. 74)
  - chapters/26-memory-mapping.md §26.4.1 'Lifecycle' bullet and §26.5 (re-attach/recreate -- defers to Ch. 74)
  - chapters/35-seqlocks-single-writer-publication.md §35.4.2 (torn-read validation, double-buffering -- the in-process primitive)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps run across chapters/*.md (case-insensitive): 'checkpoint|periodic snapshot|snapshot (interval|frequency|without)' -> Ch. 74 §74.3 table row ('journal replay length -- bounded by snapshot frequency') and lesson 2 ('take periodic state snapshots so replay starts from a recent checkpoint'), §74.4.4 ('replay from the last snapshot'), Ch. 75 §75.2 line 37, §75.4.4 line 99 ('Periodically write an index checkpoint and a state snapshot (the Ch. 74 core's state at a sequence point)'), §75.5 line 120, checklist 141 -- all one-line 'take snapshots', no mechanism; 'fork\(\)|copy.on.write|\bCOW\b' -> Ch. 26 §26.2 (MAP_PRIVATE semantics), Ch. 31 §31.4 (COW as immutable-publish pattern), Ch. 50 §50.3 code (fork to spawn the consumer) -- none about fork-based snapshots or their COW/TLB-shootdown cost; '\bRTO\b|recovery time objective' -> 0; 're-?attach' -> Ch. 26 §26.4.1 line 125, §26.5 line 146, checklist 168; Ch. 50 §50.2 line 45, §50.5 line 156, ex. 5 line 171, checklist 181 -- every one says 'design re-attach (header version/PID check -- Ch. 74)' and Ch. 74 (fully opened) has no such section: a dangling pointer from two chapters; 'EOWNERDEAD|PTHREAD_MUTEX_ROBUST|robust mutex|process.shared' -> 0 ('robust' appears only as an adjective in Ch. 36/43); 'torn.write|half.written|mid.write|died mid' -> Ch. 50 §50.5 ('a producer that died mid-write leaves the ring in an indeterminate state' -- problem stated, no solution), Ch. 63 ex. 3, Ch. 73 §73.5; 'magic|header version|layout version' -> Ch. 50 §50.4.2 sketches a header with magic/version and producer/consumer PIDs 'for liveness -- Ch. 74'. Opened Ch. 35 §35.4.2 (seqlock torn-read avoidance and double-buffering -- the in-process primitive the reattach protocol would build on) and Ch. 73 §73.2 (double-buffering for config).

## G69 — Chiplets and NUMA within the socket: AMD CCD/CCX and Infinity Fabric, Intel tiles/EMIB and mesh, SNC/NPS modes, L3-as-NUMA, and the core-to-core latency map (a ping-pong harness and heat-map) used to place queues, pinned threads and IRQs

- **Theme:** I. Hardware frontier · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** hardware-frontier#1, hardware-frontier#9
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** On the chiplet/multi-tile silicon most 2026 fleets run, a producer/consumer pair split across L3 domains pays several times the handoff latency the book's lock-free chapters optimise, and the book's topology model (socket + SMT sibling) cannot even express the problem.
- **Refined placement:** A new chapter is justified: Part II, immediately after Ch. 16 (NUMA Architecture) and before Ch. 17, e.g. 'Chiplets, Tiles & NUMA Within the Socket: the Core-to-Core Latency Map'. It is a distinct mechanism (AMD CCD/CCX + IOD + Infinity Fabric; Intel SPR/EMR/GNR tiles over EMIB and the mesh; SNC/NPS modes; L3-as-NUMA and non-inclusive victim L3) with its own Measure-it (a ~30-line rdtscp cache-line ping-pong harness over every (core_i, core_j) pair rendered as a heat-map, read for its block structure: SMT sibling / same L3 / cross-CCD-or-tile / cross-socket), its own Techniques (placing SPSC/Disruptor pairs and the feed->book->strategy->gateway chain within one L3 domain, choosing NPS/SNC mode, IRQ and NIC placement per domain, resctrl domain implications for Ch. 44) and its own Pitfalls. Ch. 16 cannot absorb it: its dual-socket Ice Lake reference box has no chiplets and the chapter is already 3,650 words. Companion edits: a short §42.4.3 'Choosing which cores -- read the core-to-core map' in Ch. 42 pointing at the new chapter; drop the harness into Appendix L; amend Ch. 7 §7.2.1's 'L3 is shared across the socket'; one-line cross-refs from Ch. 34 §34.3, Ch. 37 §37.3, Ch. 50 §50.3 ('pinned to two cores' -> 'to the right two cores'). Needs a Zen 4/5 or SPR reference box noted explicitly per the CLAUDE.md environment convention.
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps run across chapters/*.md: '\bCCD\b|\bCCX\b|chiplet|\bGMI\b|xGMI|\bEMIB\b|\bIOD\b|\bNPS[0-4]?\b|cross.CCD|L3 as (a )?NUMA|c2clat|core.to.core|inter-core latency|core pair' -> 0 in every file; 'Infinity Fabric' -> Ch. 16 §16.1 one clause ('UPI on Intel, Infinity Fabric on AMD') and §16.7 reference line; '\bSNC\b|sub-NUMA|cluster.on.die' -> Ch. 6 §6.4.1 one bullet and Appendix C C.1 one bullet ('SNC can lower local latency but complicates topology -- measure'); 'heat.?map' -> only Brendan Gregg latency heatmaps (App. E/G) and Ch. 28 Morton-order aside; 'tile' -> loop tiling only; 'ping.pong' -> coherence ping-pong (Ch. 7/30-37), futex ping-pong (Ch. 41), RDMA (Ch. 63) -- none a core-pair latency harness. Opened Ch. 7 §7.2.1 line 54: 'L1 and L2 are private to a core; L3 is shared across the socket' (the monolithic-L3 assumption); Ch. 16 §16.2.1: nodes are sockets ('NODE 0 (socket 0) ... NODE 1 (socket 1)', UPI); Ch. 16 §16.4.2: placement by node and NIC only; Ch. 42 §42.4.1 'Pin to the right logical CPU': criteria are NUMA node, SMT sibling, avoid core 0 -- no L3-domain or core-pair criterion; Ch. 43 §43.4.1: sibling detection only; Ch. 34 §34.3 / Ch. 37 §37.3 / Ch. 50 §50.3: 'producer/consumer pinned to two cores' with no guidance on which two; Ch. 57 §57.3: cross-core handoff measured as one steered-vs-not number; Appendix E: 'Cross-core cache-line transfer (HITM) ~40-100 ns' as a single range with no core-pair dependence; Appendix L L.3: pinning to one core, no topology map.

## G71 — Choosing and clocking the hot-path CPU: server vs desktop/workstation silicon (ring vs mesh L3, 5.5–6 GHz boost), ECC/memory-channel/PCIe-lane trade-offs, turbo ratio tuning, overclocking and stability validation, hybrid P/E cores and Thread Director

- **Theme:** I. Hardware frontier · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** hardware-frontier#3
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** The hot-box clock choice is the single largest lever on single-thread tick-to-trade latency (~40% between server and client SKUs), the book's 'turbo off' default actively steers readers away from common practice, and Appendix A.5's 'x86 is homogeneous' claim is wrong on current client silicon.
- **Refined placement:** A compact new chapter in Part I directly after Ch. 6 as its hardware-selection companion ('Choosing & Clocking the Hot-Path CPU'), kept tight: why single-thread latency is clock-bound (ns = cycles / f) and the measured Ch. 76-style kernel at ~3 GHz server vs ~5.5-6 GHz client/workstation clocks; ring-bus vs mesh L3 latency; what client silicon gives up (RAS ECC vs DDR5 on-die ECC, 2 vs 8-12 memory channels, PCIe lane budget for NIC + FPGA + capture) and how shops hybridise (client hot box, server feed/capture boxes); turbo-ratio tuning and overclocking with a stability-validation protocol (long soak under the real workload, error detection, thermal headroom); hybrid P/E cores and Thread Director as a pinning hazard on x86 (correcting Appendix A.5's claim). Hand the frequency-determinism mechanics (C-state exit tables, HWP/EPP, PL1/PL2, uncore) to the G72 chapter and cross-reference rather than duplicate. Fallback if the author wants the smallest footprint: a §6.4.4 'Choosing the silicon and running turbo deterministically' in Ch. 6 plus the Appendix A.5 correction and a C.1 line in Appendix C -- but hybrid-core and overclock-validation measurements would be squeezed.
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps run across chapters/*.md (case-insensitive): 'desktop|workstation|consumer (part|silicon|cpu)|client (part|silicon|cpu)' -> 0; 'overclock|\bPBO\b|Precision Boost|Curve Optimizer|turbo ratio|boost (clock|frequency)|all.core turbo' -> 0; '\bP-core|\bE-core|efficiency core|performance core|Thread Director|hybrid (core|cpu)' -> only Appendix A.5 (ARM big.LITTLE), which states 'a jitter source x86 homogeneous parts don't have' -- false for Alder Lake and later hybrid x86, so the book's model explicitly excludes hybrid client silicon; 'ring.bus|ring interconnect|\bmesh\b' -> 0; 'Core i9|Core Ultra|Ryzen 9|Xeon W|Threadripper|\bECC\b|RDIMM|UDIMM|memory channel|SKU' -> 0; 'procure|hardware selection|choos(e|ing) (a |the )?(cpu|processor|silicon|part)|bill of materials' -> 0; 'GHz' -> only the reference-machine notes (2.9 GHz base Ice Lake) and Appendix E's '~3 GHz' assumption. Opened Ch. 6 §6.2.1, §6.4.1, §6.5: turbo is treated solely as a variance source to disable ('You give up peak single-core speed to get a number you can trust'; 'many shops set a fixed frequency ... Others keep turbo but pin the governor and accept bounded variance -- measure and decide' -- one line, no method); no SKU/clock/platform choice. Appendix K.1 mentions 'dense low-latency servers with high-clock CPUs' in passing. Ch. 3 §3.4.2 fixes governor/turbo for benchmarks only.

## G73 — Controlling the hardware prefetchers: IA32_MISC_FEATURE_CONTROL (MSR 0x1A4) bits, L2 AMP/LLC/XPT prefetch, AMD prefetcher BIOS switches, msr-tools under lockdown, and the toggle experiment on the hot core vs batch cores

- **Theme:** I. Hardware frontier · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** hardware-frontier#5

- **Refined placement:** Section, not chapter: Ch. 10 new §10.4.3 'Controlling the hardware prefetchers' (IA32_MISC_FEATURE_CONTROL MSR 0x1A4 bits: L2 HW, L2 adjacent-line, DCU streamer, DCU IP; Ice Lake+/SPR L2 AMP and Xeon-SP LLC/XPT/UPI uncore prefetch; AMD L1/L2 stream + region prefetchers via BIOS; rdmsr/wrmsr -p N, the msr module under lockdown/Secure Boot, BIOS knob names) plus a §10.3 toggle experiment (hot core with an L2-resident book: prefetchers on vs off, p99.9 plus L2/L3 eviction counters, and the DDIO-pollution tie to Ch. 56); one-line cross-refs from Ch. 7 §7.2.4, Ch. 33 §33.2 (adjacent-line off => 64-byte padding suffices) and Ch. 56 §56.4; a knob row in Appendix C §C.1 (BIOS) and §C.3/§C.5 (per-core MSR, verify with rdmsr). Do together with G74's Appendix C vendor table.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Everything in the proposal is absent; keep the scope to Intel core/uncore prefetchers + AMD BIOS switches + the hot-core vs batch-core policy and the measurement. Note the msr module/lockdown caveat (msr.allow_writes=on, Secure Boot) and that per-core MSR writes must be re-applied at boot (systemd unit, ties G79).

Opened every prefetcher hit: Ch. 7 §7.2.4 (lines 90-98) names next-line/adjacent-line, stride and L2 stream prefetchers and what they can/cannot predict - no controls; Ch. 10 §10.1/§10.4.1/§10.6 treat the hardware prefetcher only as the thing software prefetch falls back from ('if the hardware already handles it, delete the prefetch'); Ch. 33 §33.2/§33.5/§33.6 cover the adjacent-line prefetcher only as a 128-byte false-sharing hazard; Ch. 56 (DDIO) has zero 'prefetch' hits; Appendix C §C.1 is seven vendor-agnostic bullets (C-states, P-states/turbo, SMT, NUMA/snoop, devices, power profile, SMIs) with no prefetcher row, and §C.5 verifies C-states/governor/isolation/mitigations but never MSR 0x1A4. Nothing teaches which prefetchers exist as controllable units, how to flip them (MSR bits, msr module under lockdown, BIOS names) or measures the hot-core on/off effect.

## G75 — The DRAM subsystem below L3: channels/ranks/bank groups, row-buffer hits vs conflicts (bimodal miss latency), loaded vs unloaded latency (MLC loaded_latency), refresh stalls, DDR5 on-die ECC, HBM, and CXL expanders/memory tiering as CPU-less NUMA nodes

- **Theme:** I. Hardware frontier · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** hardware-frontier#8, os-kernel-depth#14
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Loaded-latency and bimodal DRAM behaviour are a real hidden tail source the cache chapters stop short of, but HBM/CXL tiering is marginal for a colocated trading box today and the topic is not author-flagged.
- **Refined placement:** Prefer sections over the proposed new chapter - the material is mental model + one measurement with few C++ techniques, so a chapter would be thin at §4. Ch. 7: new §7.2.5 'Below L3: the DRAM subsystem' (channels -> DIMMs -> ranks -> bank groups -> rows; row-buffer hit vs conflict and why '~80 ns' is a bimodal ~45/100+ ns; open/closed page policy; tRFC refresh stalls; DDR5 on-die ECC; a sentence on HBM), extend §7.3 with an MLC --loaded_latency sweep showing the knee (Little's law) and a hot-core pointer-chase p99.9 with a streaming neighbour on vs off (ties Ch. 44 MBA, Ch. 71/75 writers), and a §7.6 pitfall ('no cache-miss counter changed but miss latency doubled'). Ch. 16: new §16.2.3 'CPU-less nodes: CXL expanders and memory tiering' (numactl -H distances, node access0/initiators, memory_tiers, numa_balancing=2 demotion/promotion as a page-migration noise source that can undo Ch. 46 warm-up, --membind to exclude far nodes) and a §16.5 pitfall. Appendix E: rows for DRAM row-hit vs row-conflict, loaded DRAM at the knee, and CXL-attached memory. Promote to a chapter only if the author wants HBM/CXL at depth.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Core HFT-relevant piece: bimodal DRAM miss latency and loaded vs unloaded latency under a streaming neighbour (capture/logging) - this is what the order book pays. CXL/tiering is a forward-looking NUMA hazard (low priority sub-item); HBM is background only.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 07-memory-hierarchy-and-caches.md §7.2.1 (DRAM as a single ~80 ns level)
  - 16-numa-architecture.md §16.2.1 (per-node memory controllers, NUMA factor)
  - 44-cache-allocation-intel-rdt.md §44.3, §44.4.2 (bandwidth-hungry neighbour stalling hot-path misses - stated, not measured; MBA as the fix)
  - appendix-E-latency-numbers.md §E.2 (Intel MLC named for unloaded latency)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps across chapters/*.md: 'DDR[45]' -> Appendix E §E.1 caveat only; 'DIMM|bank group|row[- ]buffer|row hit|row miss|tRFC|tCL|tRCD|loaded latency|loaded_latency|\bHBM\b|\bCXL\b|memory tier|memory_tiers|CPU-less|page policy|memory channel' -> 0; '\brank\b' -> only MPI ranks (Ch. 68) and percentile rank (Ch. 1); 'refresh' -> only feed snapshot/refresh channels (Ch. 54) and PGO profile refresh (Ch. 22); 'Little.s law' -> Ch. 1 §1.2.1 queueing only; 'MLC|Memory Latency Checker' -> Appendix E §E.2 as an unloaded-latency measurement tool; 'memory controller' -> Ch. 16 §16.2.1 ('own memory controllers + attached DRAM') and Ch. 44 §44.2/§44.4.2 ('saturate the memory controller'). Opened Ch. 7 §7.2.1: DRAM is one box '~80 ns local / ~130 ns remote' with nothing below it; Ch. 16 §16.2.1: per-node controllers and the ~1.5-2x NUMA factor; Ch. 44 §44.3: measures a cache-thrashing neighbour vs CAT and only states the bandwidth-bound-neighbour case ('MBA matters when the neighbor is bandwidth-bound... measure with MBM') - no loaded-latency curve, no bimodal row-hit/row-conflict latency, no refresh stalls, nothing on HBM, CXL expanders, CPU-less NUMA nodes or kernel memory tiering.

## G76 — Inside the scheduler: EEVDF (6.6+) vs CFS, SCHED_DEADLINE in depth (admission, CBS, GRUB), PREEMPT_RT now in mainline (6.12+) and whether a trading host should run it, threaded IRQs, stalld

- **Theme:** J. OS & kernel depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** os-kernel-depth#5
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** The hot-path scheduling recipe is complete, so this is depth for housekeeping threads and the 'should we run the RT kernel' question - valuable but specialised; the CFS->EEVDF wording fix is a correctness issue that should land regardless.
- **Refined placement:** Expand Ch. 45 rather than add a chapter - Ch. 45 keeps the recipe and gains the internals. (1) Correct every 'CFS' to 'EEVDF (CFS before 6.6)' in §45.1/§45.2/§45.3 - a factual fix independent of scope. (2) New §45.2.x 'Inside the scheduler: EEVDF lag, virtual deadlines and slice requests' (sched_setattr sched_runtime as a latency hint for housekeeping threads that share a core; removed sysctls -> /sys/kernel/debug/sched/*; wake-up preemption changes). (3) New §45.4.3 'SCHED_DEADLINE and the RT kernel: when a trading host wants them' (runtime/deadline/period, admission control vs sched_rt_runtime_us, CBS throttling, SCHED_FLAG_RECLAIM/GRUB; PREEMPT_RT in mainline 6.12+, PREEMPT_DYNAMIC, threadirqs, measured cyclictest/throughput cost, and the verdict for a busy-poll isolated box vs a shared gateway box; stalld as the RT-throttle alternative). (4) §45.5 pitfalls: forced IRQ threads landing on the wrong core; DEADLINE admission failing silently. Appendix C §C.2/§C.3 rows for threadirqs and preempt=.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Covered: isolation knobs, RT classes at recipe level, RT throttling, priority inheritance, cyclictest. Missing: EEVDF internals and knobs, SCHED_DEADLINE in depth, PREEMPT_RT/PREEMPT_DYNAMIC decision, threaded IRQs, stalld. The 'CFS' wording is a correctness bug to fix even if nothing else is added.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.2, §45.3, §45.4.2, §45.5
  - 41-context-switching-mitigation.md §41.4.1, §41.5
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps across chapters/*.md: 'EEVDF|sched_setattr|\bCBS\b|\bGRUB\b|threadirqs|irq thread|ksoftirqd|stalld|PREEMPT_DYNAMIC|virtual deadline|sched_min_granularity|wakeup_granularity|/sys/kernel/debug/sched' -> 0; 'PREEMPT_RT' -> Ch. 6 §6.9 Additional Reading and Ch. 45 §45.8 References only (no explanatory text); 'SCHED_DEADLINE' -> one sentence in Ch. 45 §45.2 ('EDF... less common for busy-polling HFT') plus the reference list; '\bCFS\b' -> Ch. 45 lines 13, 21, 42, 48, 68 describe the default scheduler as 'the CFS fair scheduler' - stale for kernels >= 6.6 where EEVDF replaced it. Opened Ch. 45 §45.2 (isolcpus/nohz_full/rcu_nocbs/irqaffinity; SCHED_OTHER/FIFO/RR/DEADLINE one paragraph each; cgroup v2 cpuset), §45.3 (cyclictest), §45.4.2 (SCHED_FIFO setup, RT throttling sched_rt_runtime_us=-1, PTHREAD_PRIO_INHERIT), §45.5 (RT throttle trap, priority inversion, isolcpus deprecation vs cpuset partitions); Ch. 41 §41.4.1/§41.5. The HFT recipe (isolate + FIFO busy-poll + disable RT throttle + PI mutexes) is genuinely developed with measurement; scheduler internals (EEVDF lag/virtual deadline, slice requests via sched_setattr), SCHED_DEADLINE depth (admission control, CBS, GRUB/SCHED_FLAG_RECLAIM, interaction with sched_rt_runtime_us), the mainline PREEMPT_RT (6.12+) decision, threaded IRQs and stalld are absent.

## G77 — Running trading workloads in VMs and containers: VM exits and EPT, posted interrupts, kvm-clock vs invariant TSC, pinned vCPUs/emulator threads, SR-IOV/VFIO passthrough, cgroup v2 cpusets and cpu.max throttling, the Kubernetes question

- **Theme:** J. OS & kernel depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** os-kernel-depth#7
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Most firms run adjacent risk/gateway/monitoring work virtualised or containerised next to the hot path and the cpu.max-throttle and VF-passthrough traps are real, but the tick-to-trade path itself is bare-metal, so this is valuable context rather than core material.
- **Refined placement:** Split by proximity to the hot host. Container half -> Ch. 45 new §45.4.3 'Containers on the trading host' (cgroup v2 cpuset partitions vs cpu.max/CFS-bandwidth throttling - measure the periodic throttle spike; cpu.weight; Kubernetes static CPU manager + topology manager and why a hot-path pod is almost never the right answer), plus a §45.5 pitfall ('cpu.max quota = periodic multi-ms stalls'). VM half -> a new short Appendix M 'Trading Workloads in Virtual Machines' beside Appendix A (which already decides cloud vs bare-metal): where exits happen (EPT violations, MSR/APIC access, HLT/PAUSE-loop exiting), posted interrupts/APICv, kvm.halt_poll_ns, huge pages at both levels (Ch. 15), -cpu host,invtsc and kvm-clock vs TSC (Ch. 17), pinned vCPUs/emulator/iothreads, SR-IOV VF passthrough via VFIO (Ch. 66), guest vs host cyclictest and kernel-stack RX measured. A new Part-VII chapter is not justified because the tick-to-trade path itself never runs in a VM.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Covered: cgroup v2 cpuset as an isolation knob, the cloud-vs-bare-metal tier decision. Missing: all KVM mechanics and guest tuning, SR-IOV/VFIO passthrough, cpu.max throttling, Kubernetes CPU/topology managers. Keep the framing 'adjacent workloads (risk, gateway with a VF, monitoring) - not the hot path'.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 42-thread-interrupt-pinning.md §42.2, §42.4.1, §42.5 (cpuset cgroups as confinement)
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.2, §45.4.1, §45.5 (cgroup v2 cpuset isolated partitions)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md §C.3 (AllowedCPUs for background services)
  - appendix-A-arm-graviton.md §A.7 (cloud vs bare-metal placement by tier)
  - 06-system-setup-for-low-latency.md §6.2.3 (multi-tenant/cloud and mitigations; 'VM exit' named)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps across chapters/*.md: '\bKVM\b|VM-exit|vmexit|\bEPT\b|\bNPT\b|posted interrupt|APICv|kvm-clock|invtsc|vcpupin|vCPU|SR-IOV|virtual function|hypervisor|virtual machine|virtualiz|docker|podman|libvirt|QEMU|Kubernetes|k8s|CPU manager|cpu\.max' -> 0 apart from 'VM exit' named once in Ch. 6 §6.2.3 (MDS/TAA flush cost) and 'VFIO' as a DPDK binding word in Ch. 62/66; 'cgroup|cpuset' -> Ch. 42 §42.2/§42.4.1/§42.5 (cpuset cgroups confine non-hot tasks; a cgroup can override sched_setaffinity), Ch. 45 §45.2/§45.4.1/§45.5 (cgroup v2 cpuset.cpus.partition=isolated; isolcpus deprecation), App. C §C.3 (systemctl set-property AllowedCPUs); 'in containers' -> Ch. 2 §2.5 (--cap-add=PERFMON for perf); 'cloud' -> Ch. 6 §6.2.3 (multi-tenant cloud = keep mitigations), App. A §A.7 (cloud vs bare-metal by tier, no mechanics). Opened each: the cgroup-v2 cpuset angle is genuinely developed as an isolation tool and App. A §A.7 settles 'the order path stays bare-metal'; nothing covers VM exit/EPT/posted-interrupt costs, kvm-clock vs invariant-TSC passthrough (Ch. 17 never mentions guests), pinned vCPU/emulator/iothreads, SR-IOV VF passthrough with VFIO, cpu.max/CFS-bandwidth throttling of containers, or Kubernetes static CPU/topology managers.

## G78 — Userspace drivers and kernel modules: resourceN mmap vs UIO vs VFIO (IOMMU groups, DMA mapping, MSI-X eventfds), a minimal C++ VFIO driver for a doorbell/completion device, and when to go kernel-side

- **Theme:** J. OS & kernel depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** os-kernel-depth#8
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Every bypass/FPGA path rests on this and Ch. 66's own measurement presupposes it, but most shops consume vendor libraries (ef_vi/DPDK/libexanic) rather than write VFIO drivers, so it is a specialised complement rather than core tick-to-trade material.
- **Refined placement:** Start as a section, not a chapter: Ch. 66 new §66.4.4 'Reaching the device from user space: resourceN, UIO and VFIO' - the three access routes and their trade-offs (no IOMMU vs IOMMU-protected; one UIO interrupt eventfd vs per-vector MSI-X eventfds), binding with driverctl / dpdk-devbind.py, IOMMU groups and ACS, iommu=pt vs IOTLB cost (ties §66.5), and a minimal C++ VFIO driver (open group/device, GET_REGION_INFO + mmap the BAR, hugepage-backed VFIO_IOMMU_MAP_DMA ring, doorbell write + host-memory completion poll) - exactly what §66.3's measurement needs; close with 'when to go kernel-side' (shared/multi-process or IRQ-heavy devices). Add Appendix I cookbook lines (lspci -vv for IOMMU groups, driverctl, dpdk-devbind -s) and a §66.3 pointer to the new section. Promote to a full chapter between Ch. 66 and 67 only if the kernel-module half gets its own worked example.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Covered: MMIO/BAR/IOMMU/doorbell/MSI-X concepts and the posted-vs-non-posted measurement. Missing: how user space actually obtains the BAR and DMA mapping (resourceN/UIO/VFIO), binding tools, IOMMU-group/ACS gotchas, MSI-X eventfd wiring, a complete minimal driver, and the kernel-module decision.

Opened Ch. 66: §66.2.2 (BARs mmap'd 'by the OS', IOMMU as a device TLB, 'VFIO uses it'), §66.2.3 (descriptor-ring + doorbell pattern), §66.3 ('requires a mapped device BAR - e.g. via a driver or DPDK/VFIO' - the reader is never shown how), §66.4.1 (write-combining/fencing), §66.4.2 (MSI-X vs polling), §66.5 (IOMMU passthrough trade), §66.7 exercise 1 (same unexplained prerequisite). ## G80 — Advanced mapping and locking tricks: the double-mapped ('magic') ring buffer via memfd, mremap, MCL_ONFAULT, MADV_POPULATE_READ/WRITE and MADV_COLLAPSE, userfaultfd, stack pre-faulting, and self-inflicted TLB shootdowns from munmap/mprotect on housekeeping threads

- **Theme:** J. OS & kernel depth · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** os-kernel-depth#10
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** The double-mapped ring and the self-inflicted TLB shootdown are two of the most practical hot-path fixes in the book's domain and sit a clause away from existing text, but the basic pre-fault/mlock discipline is already thorough and the rest (mremap, userfaultfd, MADV_COLLAPSE) is niche, so this is a high-value section rather than central missing material.
- **Refined placement:** Agree with the section placement. Ch. 26 new §26.4.3 'Beyond MAP_POPULATE: advanced mapping tricks' - (a) the double-mapped ring: memfd_create + ftruncate + two adjacent MAP_FIXED|MAP_SHARED mappings (and the MAP_HUGETLB alignment variant) so a wrapped message is contiguous, cross-referenced from Ch. 34 §34.4.2, Ch. 53 §53.4.4 and Ch. 54 (reorder buffers); (b) mremap(MREMAP_MAYMOVE|MREMAP_DONTUNMAP) for cold-path arena growth/relocation; (c) MCL_ONFAULT, MADV_POPULATE_READ/WRITE vs MAP_POPULATE vs a write loop (with kernel versions), MADV_COLLAPSE for explicit THP collapse at setup, userfaultfd as the 'never on the hot path' note. Promote the §26.5 clause to a measured pitfall: housekeeping-thread munmap/mprotect/MADV_DONTNEED -> TLB-shootdown IPI on the hot core (/proc/interrupts TLB row, hot-core p99.9 before/after) with the fix (map once, never unmap in-session; do madvise from a separate process). Ch. 23 §23.4.2: add 'pre-fault the hot thread's stack' (touch N MiB at startup, pthread_attr_setstacksize, sigaltstack) beside MAP_POPULATE. One-line cross-refs from Ch. 15 §15.5 and Ch. 41 §41.5.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Covered: MAP_POPULATE/warm-up pass, mlock/mlockall, RLIMIT_MEMLOCK, madvise hints, memfd as shm, huge-page/NUMA-aware pre-fault. Missing: double-mapped ring, mremap, MCL_ONFAULT, MADV_POPULATE_*/MADV_COLLAPSE, userfaultfd, stack pre-fault, and a measured (not one-clause) treatment of self-inflicted TLB shootdowns.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 26-memory-mapping.md §26.2, §26.3, §26.4.1, §26.4.2, §26.5 (MAP_POPULATE/mlock/madvise; memfd for sharing; one-clause shootdown pitfall)
  - 23-memory-management-fundamentals.md §23.4.1, §23.4.2
  - 15-virtual-memory-tlb-huge-pages.md §15.4.1, §15.5
  - 42-thread-interrupt-pinning.md §42.5 (shootdown IPIs as stragglers)
  - 34-lock-free-data-structures.md §34.4.2 and 37-the-disruptor-pattern.md §37.4.1 (slot-ring wrap only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Greps across chapters/*.md: 'memfd' -> Ch. 26 §26.2/§26.4.1 and Ch. 50 refs as a shm-sharing alternative only; 'double-mapp|double mapp|magic ring|mirror(ed)? mapping|mremap|MREMAP|MCL_ONFAULT|MADV_POPULATE|MADV_COLLAPSE|userfaultfd|stack.{0,15}pre-?fault' -> 0; 'TLB shootdown|shootdown' -> Ch. 26 §26.5 one clause ('Unmapping and re-mapping per use reintroduces faults (and TLB shootdowns across cores)') and Ch. 42 §42.5 ('TLB shootdowns, function-call IPIs... hard to fully eliminate'); 'MAP_POPULATE|MAP_HUGETLB|MAP_LOCKED|mlockall|MCL_' -> developed in Ch. 26 §26.2/§26.3/§26.4.2 (first-touch vs MAP_POPULATE measurement; mlock/mlockall(MCL_CURRENT|MCL_FUTURE); RLIMIT_MEMLOCK; madvise WILLNEED/HUGEPAGE/DONTNEED/RANDOM/SEQUENTIAL), Ch. 23 §23.4.2, Ch. 15 §15.4.1/§15.5, Ch. 46 §46.4.2; 'wrap|straddl|split' in Ch. 34/37/50/53/54 -> only fixed-size-slot index masking (Ch. 34 §34.4.2) and Disruptor wrap protection (Ch. 37) - byte-stream rings with variable-length messages straddling the end are never addressed. Opened Ch. 23 §23.4.1 (stack is 'one instruction, no tail' with a guard-page warning - no pre-fault of the hot thread's stack), Ch. 15 §15.5 and Ch. 41 §41.5 (no TLB-shootdown-from-housekeeping item). The basic pre-fault/mlock discipline is thoroughly taught; the double-mapped memfd ring, mremap(MREMAP_DONTUNMAP), MCL_ONFAULT, MADV_POPULATE_READ/WRITE (5.14+) vs MAP_POPULATE, MADV_COLLAPSE (6.1), userfaultfd, explicit stack pre-faulting and a measured self-inflicted TLB shootdown (housekeeping munmap/mprotect/MADV_DONTNEED -> IPI on the hot core) are missing.

## G81 — The latency cost of the kernel's own security subsystems: LSM hooks (SELinux/AppArmor), audit, seccomp and the SSBD trap, Yama/ptrace_scope, kernel lockdown — measured, with the targeted alternatives to audit=0 selinux=0

- **Theme:** J. OS & kernel depth · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** os-kernel-depth#12
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Specialised OS-hardening material whose cost mostly lands on housekeeping threads; the seccomp-implies-SSBD hazard and the misleading `audit=0 selinux=0` checklist line justify a measured section, but it is not central to building the tick-to-trade path and the author area is 'other'.
- **Refined placement:** No new chapter. Append a new §72.4.9 'The cost of the kernel's own security subsystems: LSM, audit, seccomp (and the SSBD trap), Yama, lockdown — measured' at the end of §72.4 (avoids renumbering 72.4.7/72.4.8); add three rows to the §72.3 table (SELinux/AppArmor enforcing vs permissive vs disabled per syscall on housekeeping threads; audit with rules vs `auditctl -e 0`/`-a task,never` vs `audit=0`; seccomp filter with `spec_store_bypass_disable=seccomp` vs `=prctl`); one §72.5 pitfall ('the seccomp filter silently enabled SSBD/STIBP on the hot thread'); re-point Appendix C.2 l.35 to §72.4.9 and replace `audit=0 selinux=0` with the targeted alternatives; one sentence in Ch. 6 §6.2.3 naming SSBD and the seccomp interaction.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Measure, don't assert: (1) LSM hook cost on the syscalls housekeeping threads actually make (write/sendmsg/openat) enforcing vs permissive vs `selinux=0`, plus the compliance case for keeping the LSM and confining only the trading unit (permissive domain / unconfined_t); (2) audit per-syscall cost with and without rules vs `audit=0`, and why housekeeping-tier audit is acceptable when the hot path is syscall-free (Ch. 41); (3) the SSBD trap: under `spec_store_bypass_disable=seccomp` / `spectre_v2_user=seccomp` (the default before Linux 5.16 and still in some distro kernels — check /sys/devices/system/cpu/vulnerabilities/) installing a seccomp filter sets SSBD/STIBP for the task, taxing store-heavy hot code even though it never syscalls; show `prctl` mode and `SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_SPEC_ALLOW`, measure with/without; (4) Yama `ptrace_scope` and kernel lockdown as ~zero-latency settings that instead constrain Ch. 5 record/replay debugging and Ch. 61 uprobes/`perf` — document, don't fear. Keep it ~600-900 words; the hot path itself remains syscall-free, so the lesson is 'where the cost lands and what it blocks'.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.4.6 — seccomp-bpf installed at the steady-state boundary, capability drop, namespaces (placement developed; cost asserted, not measured)
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.2.3 / §72.3 — hardening-vs-latency table (no LSM/audit/seccomp rows)
  - 06-system-setup-for-low-latency.md §6.2.3 / §6.4.3 — spec-exec mitigations and `mitigations=` (no SSBD, no seccomp interaction)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.2 l.35 — bare `audit=0 selinux=0` checklist line
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'SELinux|AppArmor|\bLSM\b|security module|auditctl|auditd|audit=0|kernel\.audit|\bSSBD\b|spec_store_bypass|speculative store bypass|Yama|ptrace_scope|lockdown|seccomp' chapters/*.md -> seccomp only in 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md (l.120-124 §72.4.6, l.155 §72.5, l.166/177 §72.6, l.184 refs) and appendix-F l.172; 'audit=0 selinux=0' only appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md l.35 ('if your security policy permits', no cost, no alternative); SELinux (elsewhere)/AppArmor/LSM/auditctl/auditd/SSBD/spec_store_bypass/Yama/ptrace_scope/lockdown: 0 hits. Opened Ch. 6 §6.2.3 l.48-56 and §6.4.3 l.117-127: retpoline/IBRS/KPTI/MDS discussed, never `spec_store_bypass_disable` or the seccomp interaction. Opened Ch. 72 §72.2.3/§72.3 l.40-62: hardening-cost table has rows for ASLR/PIE, FORTIFY, stack protector, CET, CFI, retpoline — no LSM, audit or seccomp row; §72.4.6 l.124 asserts seccomp 'near-zero hot-path cost' with no measurement.

## G82 — Anatomy of the Linux network datapath: RX/TX walkthrough with a per-stage cost column (DMA → hardirq → NAPI → GRO → IP/UDP/TCP → socket → recvmsg), softirq/ksoftirqd traps on isolated cores, qdiscs and BQL, netfilter/conntrack cost, namespaces

- **Theme:** K. Networking depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** networking-depth#2
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author area debugging-diagnostics: the book quotes 5-20 µs for the kernel stack and jumps to bypass, yet recovery channels, drop-copy and order replies ride that stack and nothing teaches which stage owns which microsecond or how to trace it; the softirq/ksoftirqd-on-isolated-core trap is an unnamed jitter source in Part VII.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part VIII immediately after Ch. 47 (before Ch. 48 io_uring), e.g. 'Anatomy of the Linux Network Datapath: Where the Microseconds Go' — the mechanism chapter that Ch. 48-52, 55, 57, 61 and 62 currently assume. A section inside Ch. 47 cannot hold an RX+TX walkthrough with a per-stage cost column plus softirq/qdisc/netfilter/namespace material. Add one pitfall to Ch. 42 §42.5 or Ch. 45 §45.5 ('NET_RX softirq / ksoftirqd landing on an isolated core') pointing at it, and re-point Appendix C.3's RPS/XPS/backlog/busy_poll knobs to its sections.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** RX stage by stage with a measured cost column: NIC DMA → MSI-X hardirq → `napi_schedule` → `net_rx_action` softirq → driver poll → GRO → `netif_receive_skb` (generic XDP/tc hooks) → IP → UDP/TCP → socket queue → `sk_data_ready` wakeup → `recvmsg` copy; attributed with ftrace `function_graph` and bpftrace on `net:*`, `skb:*`, `sock:*`, `irq:softirq_*` tracepoints anchored to the NIC hardware RX timestamp (Ch. 55/58). TX mirror: `sendmsg` → qdisc (`pfifo_fast`/`mq`/`noqueue`, where ETF/FQ of Ch. 59 plug in) → BQL/dql → driver → doorbell → TX completion. The softirq/ksoftirqd trap on isolated cores (`netdev_budget`, `netdev_budget_usecs`, RPS steering onto the wrong core, `threadirqs`). Netfilter/conntrack per-packet cost and proving the ruleset is empty / module unloaded. Network namespaces and veth as a hidden hop. Leave Ch. 47's epoll/readiness material in place; this chapter is what lies beneath the syscall.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 47-linux-native-io.md §47.2 (one-line kernel-path sketch) and §47.3 (end-to-end epoll vs busy-poll RTT, no per-stage split)
  - 55-nic-features-offloads.md §55.2 / §55.3 (interrupt coalescing, GRO/LRO, NAPI busy-poll — measured as whole-path deltas)
  - 52-advanced-tcp-internals-tuning.md §52.2 / §52.3 (GRO/LRO receive latency, delayed ACK)
  - 49-zero-copy-modern-kernel-fast-path.md §49.2 (copy points on RX/TX)
  - 61-ebpf-bpftrace-xdp.md §61.2 / §61.4.2 (XDP hook position before skb allocation)
  - 59-precise-scheduled-transmission.md §59.2 (ETF/FQ qdiscs only)
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.2 (RCU softirq offload only)
  - appendix-C-system-tuning-checklist.md C.3 (RPS/XPS, netdev_max_backlog, busy_poll knobs without mechanism)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E '\bNAPI\b|net_rx_action|ksoftirqd|softirq|netdev_budget|\bGRO\b|\bLRO\b|qdisc|\bBQL\b|byte queue|pfifo_fast|noqueue|netfilter|conntrack|iptables|nftables|netif_receive_skb|sk_data_ready|hardirq|sk_buff|\bskb\b|backlog|RPS|XPS|network namespace|netns' and 'ftrace|function_graph|trace-cmd|tracepoint:net|napi_poll|netif_receive|sys_recv' across chapters/*.md -> NAPI appears only as the 'NAPI busy-poll' option name (Ch. 41 l.135; Ch. 47 l.119/171; Ch. 55 l.30/59/83/123/134); softirq only RCU (Ch. 45 l.31/38); ksoftirqd/netdev_budget/net_rx_action/netif_receive_skb/sk_data_ready/BQL/pfifo_fast/netfilter/conntrack/netns: 0 hits; qdisc only ETF/FQ in Ch. 59; skb only Ch. 49 l.24 diagram and Ch. 61 l.41/99 ('before the kernel allocates an sk_buff'); RPS/XPS and netdev_max_backlog only as bare knobs in appendix-C l.45/47; ftrace only Ch. 45 l.75/121 (cyclictest break-trace). Opened Ch. 47 §47.2 l.47-52: one-line 'NIC IRQ → driver → IP/TCP → socket buffer → epoll_wait → read() → copy' sketch; §47.3 l.61-109 measures only end-to-end loopback RTT (~5-6 µs blocking vs ~3 µs busy-poll). Ch. 55 §55.3 l.57-59 is a whole-path table (default ~8 µs / GRO+coalescing off ~5 µs / busy-poll ~3 µs), Ch. 52 §52.3 l.50 GRO on/off — stage effects measured, no attribution method.

## G83 — Line-rate packet capture and timing-faithful replay: capture-point fidelity (in-app vs AF_PACKET vs NIC mirror vs SPAN vs optical tap vs capture cards), TPACKET_V3/PF_RING/rte_pdump, replaying the open at original cadence; passive wire-level measurement appliances (Corvil/Pico-style, timestamped mirrors)

- **Theme:** K. Networking depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** networking-depth#5, observability-depth#9
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** Author area debugging-diagnostics: the book's own exercises in Ch. 53, 55 and 60 depend on replaying a captured open onto the wire, which nothing teaches, and independent tap-based wire measurement is how a desk verifies the application's own latency numbers — both exist only as a sentence.
- **Refined placement:** New compact chapter in Part XII between Ch. 75 and Ch. 76 ('Line-Rate Packet Capture & Timing-Faithful Replay') owning the capture point, the host capture stack, and the wire-replay harness that the Ch. 53/55/60 exercises assume; plus a short new §60.4.4 'Passive measurement: taps vs SPAN, timestamped mirrors and latency appliances' in Ch. 60 carrying the SPAN-vs-tap timestamp-error measurement, with pointers from Ch. 75 §75.4.1 and Ch. 58 §58.3. Fallback if no new chapter: expand Ch. 75 §75.4.1 (host stacks with code) and §75.4.5 (replay onto the wire) plus §60.4.4 — but that pushes Ch. 75 from 3,852 to ~5,500+ words.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** (1) Capture-point fidelity table: in-app journal vs host `AF_PACKET` vs NIC mirror vs switch SPAN/ERSPAN vs passive optical tap + layer-1 replicator vs capture cards/appliances (Napatech, Endace, ExaNIC, Arista 7130 MetaWatch, Corvil/Pico-style analytics) — what each loses (drops under oversubscription, timestamp jitter, reordering, encapsulation); measure SPAN-vs-tap timestamp error on the same stream. (2) Host capture at line rate: `TPACKET_V3` mmap rings + `PACKET_FANOUT`, PF_RING ZC, DPDK `rte_pdump`/pcapng, AF_XDP capture (Ch. 61), hardware timestamp trailers and pcap-ng ns timestamps; proving losslessness at the open with `ethtool -S` and ring counters. (3) Timing-faithful replay onto the wire: tcpreplay limits, DPDK/MoonGen/TRex pacing from pcap timestamps, hardware-timed replay via `SO_TXTIME`/LAUNCHTIME (Ch. 59) to reproduce microburst cadence into the receiver under test; validate replay fidelity with the tap. Ch. 75 keeps journals/storage; Ch. 76 keeps state-machine replay and the CI gate.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md §75.2 (two capture altitudes), §75.3 (off-NIC capture ~0 app cost in the comparison table), §75.4.1 (one sentence naming tap / SPAN / NIC RX capture / SmartNIC, `AF_PACKET`/`PF_RING`, 'a dedicated capture appliance'), §75.4.5 (replay into the deterministic core only)
  - 60-network-fabric-switching.md §60.3 (passive tap for per-hop measurement), §60.4.3 (switch hardware timestamping, SPAN/layer-1 replication for capture, aggregation taps)
  - 70-smartnics-dpus.md §70.3 / §70.5 (wire-to-wire via PTP-timestamped tap), §70.4.2 (capture offload onto the DPU)
  - appendix-I-tooling-cookbook.md I.8 (tcpdump inter-packet-delta one-liner)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'TPACKET|PACKET_FANOUT|PACKET_RX_RING|packet_mmap|AF_PACKET|PF_RING|pdump|ERSPAN|optical tap|passive tap|SPAN|mirror port|tcpreplay|Napatech|Endace|Corvil|Instrumentix|MetaWatch|Metamako|timestamp trailer|pcap|libpcap|tcpdump|capture card|capture appliance' chapters/*.md -> AF_PACKET/PF_RING only 75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md l.69 and l.129 (named, no code, no TPACKET_V3/FANOUT); pdump/tcpreplay/ERSPAN/Endace/Corvil/Instrumentix/timestamp trailer/pcap-ng: 0; Napatech only as FPGA-NIC vendor (Ch. 69 l.75/136, Ch. 70 l.13/32); Metamako only as cut-through switch vendor (Ch. 60 l.33); SPAN/mirror only Ch. 60 l.81 and Ch. 75 l.52/60/69; tcpdump only Ch. 62 l.17/116/131 ('you lose it') and appendix-I l.137 one-liner; 'passive tap' Ch. 60 l.47 and Ch. 70 l.59/137 as a measurement point. Opened Ch. 75 §75.4.5 l.102-109: replay goes into the Ch. 74 state machine only, never onto the wire. grep 'replay' in Ch. 53/55/60: Ch. 53 l.59/180/225/237/246, Ch. 55 l.118/129, Ch. 60 l.56/101 all say 'replay a captured market-open microburst (Ch. 75)' — a dependency Ch. 75 does not fulfil.

## G84 — Layer-2/3 hygiene and redundancy engineering for the order and feed paths: ARP/ND stalls and permanent neighbours, DNS off the hot path, routing, MTU/jumbo trade-offs, VLAN/QoS marking; dual-NIC/dual-switch A/B design, bonding modes and failover timing, gratuitous ARP

- **Theme:** K. Networking depth · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** networking-depth#8, networking-depth#9
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Operationally real — an ARP probe or a 30 s bond failover can stall the first order after a quiet spell while resting orders are exposed — but it is network-operations hygiene rather than the C++/latency core, and the merged item is tagged 'other' (only the hygiene source block claimed health-monitoring-os-net).
- **Refined placement:** Sections, not a chapter: (a) new §51.4.3 'Network-layer hygiene: ARP/ND, DNS, routing, MTU and VLAN/QoS marking' in Ch. 51 (3,434 words, room); (b) new §60.4.4 'Redundant paths: dual-NIC/dual-switch A/B design, bonding modes and failover timing' in Ch. 60 (2,998 words, the shortest networking chapter) with a matching §60.5 pitfall and checklist items, cross-referenced from Appendix K.6 (which stays conceptual); (c) one bullet in Ch. 46 §46.4.2 — pre-resolve and pin gateway/recovery-server neighbours (`ip neigh replace … nud permanent`) as part of warming; (d) Appendix C.3 lines for `base_reachable_time_ms`, `arp_ignore`/`arp_announce`, MTU, bond `miimon`/`arp_interval`.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Hygiene: unresolved or stale neighbour → first packet queued until the ARP/ND reply (ms if the gateway is slow); `base_reachable_time_ms` re-probing mid-session; permanent entries for exchange gateways and recovery servers; `arp_ignore`/`arp_announce` on multi-NIC hosts; gratuitous ARP on failover; IPv6 ND where mandated; DNS strictly at setup (no `getaddrinfo` on reconnect — resolve once, pin IPs); MTU/jumbo trade-off (a 9000-byte snapshot frame's serialization at a cut-through egress vs fewer packets; PMTU blackholes); VLAN tag cost and whether 802.1p/DSCP marking is honoured by colo switches. Redundancy: do not bond market-data NICs — line A on NIC0→switch A, line B on NIC1→switch B with separate queues/IRQs/cores (Ch. 42/57) and PTP paths (Ch. 58), since a bond hides the per-line gap statistics Ch. 54 needs; for order entry/management: active-backup vs 802.3ad LACP (a single flow cannot be split; `xmit_hash_policy`), `fail_over_mac`, `miimon`/`arp_interval` detection time (defaults of 100 ms-30 s while orders rest), MLAG on the switch side; measure failover wire-to-wire by pulling a link.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.6 (three conceptual bullets: diverse A/B paths, redundant cross-connects/gateways, latency-vs-resilience — adjacent mention only)
  - 46-keeping-the-hot-path-warm.md §46.4.2 (pre-establish/heartbeat TCP connections, warm NIC path — the ARP/neighbour-staleness sibling is absent)
  - 60-network-fabric-switching.md §60.4.2 (symmetric paths; isolating market-data from order-entry paths)
  - 62-kernel-bypass-userspace-networking.md §62.2 (ARP/routing named as services a userspace stack must replace)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -E '\bARP\b|neighbour|\bnud\b|getaddrinfo|gethostbyname|\bDNS\b|resolv\.conf|\bMTU\b|jumbo|PMTU|\bVLAN\b|802\.1[Qp]|\bDSCP\b|\bQoS\b|bonding|bond[0-9]|teaming|\bLACP\b|802\.3ad|active-backup|miimon|gratuitous|\bMLAG\b|dual-NIC|dual NIC|dual-switch|two switches|second NIC' chapters/*.md -> ARP only 62-kernel-bypass-userspace-networking.md l.34 (as a kernel service bypass loses); VLAN only as an n-tuple/`rte_flow` match field (Ch. 57 l.34/68); QoS only Intel RDT 'Platform QoS' (Ch. 44); 802.1Q* only PFC/TSN (Ch. 59/64); DNS/getaddrinfo/MTU/jumbo/PMTU/DSCP/bonding/LACP/active-backup/miimon/gratuitous/MLAG/dual-NIC: 0 hits. grep -i 'failover|fail over|fail-over' -> Ch. 74 §74.3 (process restart), Ch. 65 l.64 (Aeron Cluster), Ch. 76 l.90/124 (simulate failover), appendix-K l.30 (microwave→fiber) and l.57 (one bullet: redundant cross-connects/gateways). Opened Ch. 46 §46.4.2 l.125-131: warms TCP handshake/slow-start and the NIC path, never the neighbour cache. Ch. 51 l.41/105: `IP_ADD_MEMBERSHIP`/`IP_MULTICAST_IF` only. Nothing develops ARP/ND stalls, DNS, MTU, VLAN/QoS marking, bonding modes or failover timing.

## G85 — Expand Ch. 60 and Ch. 55: link speed, RS-FEC/PAM4 latency (why 10G NRZ can beat 25/100G for small frames), layer-1/FPGA switch applications, in-switch timestamping and risk; NIC selection by measured latency class, firmware/driver/module-parameter version management and a per-NIC baseline re-run after every change

- **Theme:** K. Networking depth · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** networking-depth#10, networking-depth#11
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** Both halves are specialised infrastructure/ops material rather than C++ hot-path technique, but Ch. 60 currently recommends 25/100G links that, with mandatory RS-FEC, can be slower than 10G for small frames — a misleading recommendation worth correcting — and unmanaged NIC firmware/driver drift is a real silent latency-regression source.
- **Refined placement:** Expand existing, as proposed. Ch. 60: new §60.2 sub-topic 'Link speed, FEC and PAM4: the hidden per-hop cost' with the small-frame arithmetic (300-byte frame: 10G NRZ no-FEC ≈ 240 ns serialization vs 25G/100G + mandatory RS-FEC ≈ +100-250 ns + PAM4 DSP), when FEC can be disabled (short CA-N DACs, `ethtool --set-fec off`) and when the exchange handoff dictates it; correct §60.4.1, §60.5 and the checklist from 'uniform and high (25/100 G)' to 'match the exchange handoff, prefer no-FEC links for small frames, measure'; extend §60.4.3 with layer-1/FPGA switch applications (muxing/aggregation, in-switch timestamping, in-switch pre-trade risk). Ch. 55: new §55.4.4 'Choosing the NIC: measured latency classes and feature matrix' and §55.4.5 'Firmware, driver and configuration management' (`ethtool -i`, in-tree vs OFED/Onload/DDP bundles, DKMS and kernel-upgrade regression risk, module parameters that move latency: mlx5 CQE compression/striding RQ, Intel ITR/adaptive moderation, sfc/Onload `EF_*` spin settings, persisted config), plus a §55.3 'per-NIC baseline recipe you re-run after every change' tied to Ch. 76 §76.4.1 and Appendix C.3.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The FEC/PAM4 correction is a factual fix to existing guidance and should be made even if the rest is deferred. Keep NIC selection vendor-neutral and measured (latency class by wire-to-app ns under one harness, Appendix L); make the baseline a pinned kernel+driver+firmware triple with a stored per-NIC histogram diffed in CI; add transceiver/DAC vs AOC vs optics latency to K.2 or §60.2.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 60-network-fabric-switching.md §60.2 (serialization delay = frame_bits/link_rate; 'link speed matters'), §60.4.1 ('Keep link speeds uniform and high (25/100 G)'), §60.4.3 (switch timestamping/replication/aggregation taps), §60.5 + checklist ('mismatched link speeds')
  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.2 (fiber vs copper/DAC propagation, transceiver cost — no FEC)
  - 55-nic-features-offloads.md §55.3 (offload on/off latency table), §55.4.3 (Onload/ef_vi/libexanic), §55.7 (vendor tuning guides)
  - 45-realtime-scheduling-kernel-tuning.md §45.5 ('re-run cyclictest after any kernel/BIOS change' — host-level analog of the per-NIC baseline)
  - 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md §76.4.1 (latency-regression gates, software side only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -E '\bFEC\b|RS-FEC|forward.error|PAM4|\bNRZ\b|set-fec|\bDAC\b|\bAOC\b|transceiver|\bSFP|QSFP|link speed' chapters/*.md -> FEC/RS-FEC/PAM4/NRZ/set-fec/AOC/SFP/QSFP: 0 hits; DAC/transceiver only appendix-K l.21/23 ('copper (twinax/DAC) comparable', 'transceiver cost'); link speed only 60-network-fabric-switching.md l.38/41/67/92/108 and appendix-F l.135, all 'keep link speeds uniform and high (25/100 G)'. grep -i 'ethtool -i|driver version|NIC firmware|DKMS|out-of-tree|in-tree|kernel module|modprobe|module param|CQE|striding|rx_irq_mod|EF_[A-Z_]|OFED|DOCA|DDP|i40e|mlx5|\bsfc\b|ixgbe|re-?run.{0,30}(baseline|after)|baseline.{0,40}(NIC|driver|firmware)' -> firmware only in BIOS context (Ch. 6 l.92-102, Ch. 45, appendix-C); CQE only io_uring (Ch. 48); DOCA only Ch. 70 as an SDK; no NIC driver/firmware/module-parameter management anywhere; the only 're-run after any change' discipline is Ch. 45 §45.5 l.110 for `cyclictest`. Opened Ch. 55 §55.4.3 l.88-96 and §55.7 l.131-137 (vendor bypass stacks + pointers to vendor tuning guides) and §55.3 l.36-65 (one-off offload on/off table, not a re-runnable per-NIC baseline).

## G86 — Expand Appendix K.3: RF link engineering (capacity, modem FEC/interleaving, regeneration per hop, fade behaviour) and arbitrating heterogeneous-latency copies of the same feed (microwave vs fiber)

- **Theme:** K. Networking depth · **Proposed kind:** expand-existing-chapter · **Sources:** networking-depth#12
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** low

No chapter.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Keep Appendix K's stated stance ('orientation, below the C++ layer'): the only software deliverables are the compaction encoder (top-of-book deltas for a symbol subset — ties Ch. 53/65), the fade detector, and the fast/slow arbitration policy — each a paragraph with a cross-reference, not a benchmark. ~400-600 words total.

  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.3 (three conceptual bullets: propagation 0.99c vs 0.67c, bandwidth/weather trade-off with hybrid failover, mm-wave/FSO/hollow-core)
