- **Why this priority:** Well-motivated and squarely in Part V's remit, but specialised to FP-heavy strategies (options quoting, signal math); the book's own hot-path stance is scaled-integer price math, so this is valuable rather than central, and the author area is 'other'.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part V immediately after Ch. 27 (before Ch. 28), e.g. 'Fast Transcendentals & Approximate Math for Pricing and Signals'; prerequisites Ch. 11 (dependency chains), Ch. 27, Ch. 29; leads into Ch. 67. Ch. 27 is already the largest Part V chapter (4,206 words) and cannot hold approximation design plus a worked example as a section. Turn the `vrcpps`/Newton remark in §27.5 and the App. D.3 `-fno-math-errno` bullet into cross-references to the new chapter; add exp/log/erf rows to Appendix E.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Measured glibc `exp`/`log`/`pow`/`erf`/`tanh` scalar latency, throughput and tail on the reference box (errno path, special-case branches, denormal inputs); `-fno-math-errno`/`-fno-trapping-math` effect on inlining; range reduction + minimax (Remez) vs Taylor, Horner vs Estrin and their dependency-chain depth (Ch. 11), FMA evaluation, degree-vs-error tables, bounding absolute/relative/ULP error; table-driven exp/log/erf with `constexpr` tables (Ch. 18) and their cache footprint (Ch. 7); vectorized libraries (glibc libmvec, SLEEF, SVML/oneMKL VM) and when they pay (Ch. 29); determinism across builds and replay (Ch. 27 §27.4.3, Ch. 75); worked example: Black-Scholes price/Greeks and an implied-vol solve (Newton vs rational initial guess) at tens of ns per quote; verify codegen (no `call exp`, FMA chain shape).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.2.4 and §27.3 (FP op latency table: add/mul/FMA vs div/sqrt; keep div/sqrt off dependency chains; reciprocal-multiply hoisting)
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.5 (`vrcpps` + Newton refinement shown as a `-ffast-math` artifact; FMA contraction vs determinism)
  - appendix-D-compiler-flag-reference.md D.3 (`-fno-math-errno` lets `sqrt` inline to one instruction; `-ffast-math` sub-flags)
  - 67-gpu-computing-cuda.md §67.4.3 (Black-Scholes/Greeks/Monte-Carlo as off-hot-path GPU work only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'polynomial|minimax|remez|horner|estrin|\berf\b|erfc|normal cdf|cumulative normal|\blibm\b|libmvec|\bSVML\b|SLEEF|Black.Scholes|implied.vol|\bGreeks?\b|fno-math-errno|std::exp\b|std::log\b|std::pow\b|\bexp\(|\btanh|range reduction|approximat(e|ion) (math|exp|log|sqrt|reciprocal)|fast (exp|log|math)|transcendental|EWMA|log.return|vrcp|rsqrt|Newton' chapters/*.md -> polynomial/minimax/Remez/Horner/Estrin/erf/libm/libmvec/SVML/SLEEF/implied vol/range reduction/transcendental: 0 hits; Black-Scholes and Greeks only 67-gpu-computing-cuda.md l.13/107/112/143 (as off-path GPU batch work) and Ch. 19 l.158 (expression templates); EWMA only Ch. 27 l.42 (as a denormal source); `-fno-math-errno` only appendix-D l.32 (one bullet: lets `sqrt` inline), appendix-D l.91 build line, Ch. 22 l.124; `vrcpps`+Newton only Ch. 27 §27.5 l.177-181 as a `-ffast-math` side effect. Opened Ch. 27 §27.2.4 l.51-60 and §27.3 l.95-120: add/mul/FMA vs div/sqrt latency table and 'hoist or reciprocal-multiply' — the only developed approximate-math angle; no exp/log/pow cost, no approximation design, no error bounds, no vector math library.

## G88 — Fast hashing, checksums and key design for the hot path: hash functions vs tables, identity/multiplicative/xxHash3/wyhash/SipHash measured, fixed-width symbols as uint64 keys, perfect hashing for instrument tables, CRC32C/PCLMUL checksums, hash-flooding defence

- **Theme:** L. Algorithms & numerics · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** algorithms-numerics#2
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium (was high)
- **Why this priority:** Every message does a key lookup, but Ch. 25 already delivers the dominant layout win and a reader using its recommended libraries gets a sound default hash; the remaining gains (key design, identity hashing, CRC32C, flooding defence) are real but second-order and fit in sections rather than a chapter.
- **Refined placement:** Smallest footprint is sections, not a chapter: (a) Ch. 25 — insert a new §25.4.2 'Choosing the hash function and designing the key' after §25.4.1 (renumber 25.4.2-25.4.4 → 25.4.3-25.4.5): name the `0x9E3779B97F4A7C15` multiplier (Fibonacci hashing), identity/direct-index hashing for dense exchange-assigned IDs, fixed-width symbols loaded as `uint64` keys (one `cmp`, no `std::string`), a measured ns/hash + probe-length table for identity / multiply-xorshift / wyhash-rapidhash / xxHash3 / SipHash / `_mm_crc32_u64`-as-hash, and the perfect-hash option (pointer to §18.4.2); (b) Ch. 28 — new §28.4.5 'Checksums and hash finalizers as integer tricks' (`_mm_crc32_u64` CRC32C, PCLMUL-based CRC, splitmix/murmur finalizers) with codegen; (c) Ch. 72 §72.2.1/§72.5 — one bullet on hash flooding from attacker-controlled keys (client ClOrdIDs) and when a keyed hash is warranted; (d) Ch. 62 §62.4.2 and Ch. 75 §75.4.2 — one bullet each on trusting/verifying NIC checksum flags in a userspace stack and per-record CRC32C in the journal. A standalone Part V chapter (after Ch. 28) is justified only if the author wants the full measured shootout and perfect-hash construction (~3,500 words).
- **What is missing / scope notes:** The book already teaches the dominant lesson (layout, Ch. 25); what is missing is the fixed per-message cost paid before layout matters. Key design (symbol→`uint64`, dense ID→direct index) is the biggest practical win and belongs in Ch. 25 beside the LOB case study's orderID→Order map; hash-function choice is second-order given a good library default; CRC32C/PCLMUL and flooding defence are short, concrete additions (~1,500-2,000 words total across the four touch points).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 25-hotpath-hostile-stl-cache-friendly-containers.md §25.3 (unordered_map vs flat map measured; toy FlatMap with an unnamed Fibonacci multiplier), §25.4.1 (probing schemes, load factor, production libraries — table layout only), §25.4.4 (tick-indexed level arrays, flat orderID→Order map)
  - 18-compile-time-mechanics.md §18.4.2 (compile-time perfect hash / sorted constexpr array for closed key sets — one bullet; CRC table as the constexpr example)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.4.1 (8-byte in-place loads with byteswap — the mechanism behind a uint64 symbol key)
  - 55-nic-features-offloads.md §55.2 / §55.5 (keep NIC checksum offload on)
  - 28-bit-manipulation-integer-tricks.md §28.4.3 (power-of-two masks for hash capacity)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** grep -n -i -E 'xxhash|wyhash|rapidhash|siphash|murmur|cityhash|fnv|ahash|komihash|fibonacci hash|9E3779B9|golden ratio|perfect hash|cmph|pthash|gperf|CRC32|_mm_crc32|PCLMUL|carry-less|hash flood|hash.?dos|collision attack|hash function|hash quality|avalanche|checksum|identity hash|multiplicative hash|std::hash' chapters/*.md -> named hash functions, CRC32C/`_mm_crc32`, PCLMUL, hash flooding, avalanche, hash quality, `std::hash`: 0 hits; `0x9E3779B97F4A7C15` appears in 25-hotpath-hostile-stl-cache-friendly-containers.md l.65/70 (toy FlatMap multiplier) unnamed and undiscussed; 'perfect hash' only 18-compile-time-mechanics.md l.15/163 (one bullet); 'CRC' only Ch. 18 l.13/28/56 as a `constexpr` table example and Ch. 60 (switch CRC check); 'checksum' only as a NIC offload to keep on (Ch. 55 l.26/42/106/122, Ch. 70 l.13) and benchmark accumulators (Ch. 2/11). grep in Ch. 72/62/75 for hash|checksum|crc|integrity|DoS|flood -> no hash-flooding item in Ch. 72, no checksum verification in Ch. 62 §62.4.2/§62.5, no record CRC in Ch. 75 §75.4.2. Ch. 25 §25.3 l.45 'Look up symbols (or order IDs)' uses uint64 keys without explaining key design; Ch. 53 §53.4.1 l.87-94 shows 8-byte memcpy+byteswap loads (mechanism, not the key lesson).

## G89 — Branch-free parsing and formatting: integers, decimals to scaled integers, timestamps, FIX tag scanning — from_chars vs SWAR vs SIMD measured on one FIX message; to_chars and digit-pair formatting for the encode path

- **Theme:** L. Algorithms & numerics · **Proposed kind:** new-chapter · **Sources:** algorithms-numerics#3
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** FIX order entry sits on the tick-to-trade path at most venues, Ch. 76 budgets an 'order encode' stage that no chapter develops, and Ch. 53's own §53.3 conclusion ('parsed with optimized field-scanning + branch-free number conversion') promises code the book never shows — a central, measurable C++ hot-path topic with zero SWAR/SIMD parse code anywhere in 76 chapters.
- **Refined placement:** New chapter in Part IX immediately after Ch. 53 (becoming Ch. 54 via the repo's scripted renumber; Ch. 54-76 shift by one): 'Text Protocols on the Hot Path: Branch-Free Parsing & Formatting'. Rationale: the scope is chapter-sized (a measured 6-variant parse spectrum on one FIX message, SWAR + SIMD code, decimal/implied-decimal to scaled integer, UTCTimestamp parse, SOH/tag scanning, checksum, plus the encode path with to_chars/digit-pair/fixed-width/checksum and its own measurement), Ch. 53 is already the longest chapter at 5,218 words and is centered on binary overlay + A/B recovery + microbursts, and the encode half has no home anywhere. Builds on Ch. 13/27/28/29; feeds Ch. 71, 74 and the Ch. 76 'order encode' stage. Fallback if the author refuses a renumber: expand §53.4.1 into a full parse section with code and a §53.3 row-set (from_chars / digit-loop / SWAR-8 / SIMD-16), and add a new §53.4.5 'Encoding outbound orders' — accepting a ~7,500-word Ch. 53.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Keep what Ch. 53 already has (binary overlay, bounds checks, A/B) out of scope. Must add: (1) the parse spectrum measured on one FIX NewOrderSingle/ExecutionReport — strtoll, from_chars, digit-at-a-time with [[likely]], 8-digit SWAR multiply-shift (Lemire/Kelley), 16-digit pmaddubsw/pmaddwd chain — with branch-miss counters for variable-width vs fixed-width fields; (2) decimal price -> int64 at fixed scale and ITCH implied-decimal fields without double, rejecting malformed input at the gate (ties Ch. 72); (3) FIX UTCTimestamp 'YYYYMMDD-HH:MM:SS.sss' -> ns and back; (4) tag scanning: SIMD SOH find (pcmpeqb+movemask+tzcnt, the Ch. 29 Ex. 5 idea done properly), tag-switch as jump table, BodyLength/CheckSum handling; (5) encode path: to_chars vs digit-pair table vs fixed-width zero-padded fields, pre-built message templates with patched fields, checksum computation, measured ns per message; (6) codegen verification of the SWAR path; (7) cross-ref Ch. 76's ~120 ns encode budget so the stage finally has a chapter. The merged search terms are all correct; add 'pcmpeqb', 'movemask', 'UTCTimestamp', 'CheckSum' to them.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.3 (from_chars FIX decode benchmark, L97-139)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.4.1 (five one-line bullets, L143-151)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.6 pitfalls (atoi/stringstream, price via double, L222-223)
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.2.1 (FIX/FAST protocol description, L28-31)
  - 18-compile-time-mechanics.md §18.4.2 L160 (digit-pair constexpr table named)
  - 29-simd-vectorization.md Exercise 5 L186 (SIMD delimiter scan preview)
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.4.1 L131 (parse wire price straight to scaled integer — defers to Ch. 53)
  - 71-zero-overhead-logging.md §71.2/§71.4.2 (deferred formatting for logs only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Ch. 53 §53.3 decode.cpp (L97-106) is the only parsing code: a from_chars-based FIX field scanner measured at ~80-200 ns/msg vs ~8-15 ns for binary overlay (L131-139), and the prose then tells the reader to use 'optimized field scanning + branch-free number conversion (§53.4.1)' without showing it. §53.4.1 (L143-151) is five one-sentence bullets: from_chars/to_chars, 'parse 8 digits at once with a few multiplies/shifts' (named, no code), decimal '123.45 -> 1234500 at scale 10^4' (named, no code), 'to_chars + digit-pair tables' (named), fast_float (named). Greps I ran across chapters/*.md: `from_chars|to_chars` -> Ch. 53 (14 lines, all §53.3 code/§53.4.1 bullets/§53.6/§53.7), Ch. 72 L79/L171, App. H L76, Ch. 18 L160, Ch. 27 L131, README; `swar|8 digits|eight digits|pmaddubsw|pmaddwd|digit.pair|fast_float|SOH|tag=value` -> Ch. 53 L28/L104-113/L148-151, Ch. 18 L160, Ch. 29 L15, App. G L41, App. J L57 (all mentions); SWAR/SIMD-parse constants `0x0F0F0F0F|0x000F000F|2561|6553601|0x3030303030303030|0x0101010101010101|haszero` -> 0 files (no SWAR or SIMD digit-parse code exists anywhere); `implied.decimal|strtol|strtoll|atoi` -> atoi only in benchmark argv parsing + Ch. 53 L147/L222 'don't use atoi'; timestamp parse/format `UTCTimestamp|SendingTime|TransactTime|HH:MM|YYYYMMDD|strftime|gmtime` -> 0 substantive hits (Ch. 17 L151 and Ch. 71 L38/L73 only say 'stamp, don't format'); FIX framing `checksum|BodyLength|tag 10|tag 9` in Ch. 53 -> 0 hits. Encode side: Ch. 76 L51 budgets an 'Order encode -> TX' stage at ~120 ns p50 / ~250 ns p99.9 and names Ch. 53/62 as governing, but grep `encode|serializ|NewOrderSingle|35=D|outbound.*format` in Ch. 53/62/74/76/App. J finds only Ch. 53 L150 ('to_chars for outbound formatting ... off the hot path where possible') — no chapter develops synchronous order encoding. Ch. 29 L186 Exercise 5 (SIMD delimiter scan 'preview Ch. 53') is an exercise, not a technique section. Ch. 71 develops deferred/binary formatting for logs, which is the opposite discipline from the order-encode path that cannot be deferred.

## G90 — Searching and sorting small arrays: branchless (cmov) binary search, Eytzinger layout, prefetched search, SIMD scan for ≤64 elements, sorting networks — discharging Ch. 3's promise that Ch. 25 revisits the sorted-vector vs hash-map comparison

- **Theme:** L. Algorithms & numerics · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** algorithms-numerics#4
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** high
- **Why this priority:** The book contains an explicit, undischarged promise (Ch. 3 L154) that Ch. 25 revisits the sorted-vector-vs-hash-map comparison, and sorted small-array lookup (tick bands, symbol tables, the LOB's sparse depth store, venue ranking) is a hot-path primitive whose branchless form is the canonical application of the Ch. 13 cmov material the book sets up but never applies — a small section fixes both.
- **Refined placement:** Section, not chapter: Ch. 25 new §25.4.5 'Searching small sorted arrays: branchless binary search, Eytzinger layout and SIMD scan', placed after the LOB case study so the heavily cross-referenced §25.4.4 keeps its number, with a forward reference added to the LOB's 'secondary (sparse/flat_map) structure' bullet. Add a second measurement block to §25.3 (sorted vector std::lower_bound vs cmov-branchless vs Eytzinger vs flat hash at N = 8 / 64 / 4096 with uniform and price-level-clustered keys, with branch-misses and L1 misses) that explicitly closes Ch. 3 §3.3's promise, one §25.5 pitfall (branchless search on a predictable key stream / Eytzinger's rebuild-on-insert cost), one §25.6 exercise, and a cross-ref from Ch. 3 L154 to '§25.3/§25.4.5'. Update the README outline for Ch. 25. About 1,000-1,400 words; no renumbering.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** In scope: (1) why std::lower_bound mispredicts ~log2(n) times on unpredictable keys and serialises on dependent loads (ties Ch. 11/13); (2) the cmov half-interval form (Khuong & Morin) with fixed iteration count for known sizes, verified in asm (ties §13.5) and its dependency-chain cost on predictable data (§13.6); (3) prefetching both candidate halves one level ahead (ties Ch. 10); (4) Eytzinger/BFS layout for cache-line locality and its trade-off (static, rebuild on insert — fine for tick-size bands, symbol tables, venue lists; not for a mutating book); (5) SIMD compare+movemask+tzcnt linear scan for <= 32-64 keys as the usually-fastest option at LOB-sparse-store sizes (ties Ch. 28/29); (6) where each wins vs the flat hash map, discharging Ch. 3's promise. Keep sorting networks to a short paragraph or drop them — the trading uses (ranking <= 16 venue quotes for a consolidated BBO) are thin and the analyst's evidence shows no reader pull; do not add S-tree/B-tree layouts beyond a sentence.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 03-microbenchmarking-done-right.md §3.3 bench_lookup.cpp L85-154 (std::binary_search vs unordered_map measured; explicit undischarged promise that Ch. 25 revisits it)
  - 25-hotpath-hostile-stl-cache-friendly-containers.md §25.4.2 L127-129 (binary search named; sorted vector + lower_bound fallback named)
  - 25-hotpath-hostile-stl-cache-friendly-containers.md §25.4.4 (LOB defers deep levels to an unanalysed 'sparse/flat_map' secondary store)
  - 13-branch-prediction-branchless.md §13.4.1, §13.5, §13.6 L127-229 (cmov mechanism and its dependency-chain trap, not applied to search)
  - 18-compile-time-mechanics.md §18.4.2 L163 (sorted constexpr array with binary search, one clause)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** The 'why std::lower_bound is slow' angle exists with a measurement: Ch. 3 §3.3 bench_lookup.cpp (L85-154) measures std::binary_search on a 4096-element sorted vector (31.2 ns) losing to unordered_map (18.7 ns), explains it as '~12 dependent, branchy, cache-unfriendly comparisons' with branch-miss/L1-miss counters, and then states verbatim (L154): 'The order-book case study (Ch. 25) revisits exactly this comparison under realistic access patterns and reaches a more nuanced conclusion.' Ch. 25 never does: §25.3 (L45-115) measures only unordered_map vs a flat open-addressing hash; §25.4.2 L127 has one phrase ('Lookup is a binary search over a contiguous array') and L129 names 'a hand-maintained sorted std::vector with std::lower_bound' as the C++17 fallback; §25.4.4 the LOB case study picks a tick-indexed flat array and sends deep levels to 'a secondary (sparse/flat_map) structure' with no analysis of how that store is searched. Greps I ran: `binary.search|lower_bound|upper_bound|partition_point|binary_search` -> Ch. 1 L167 and Ch. 3 L85/L131/L154 (benchmark/percentile demos), Ch. 18 L163 ('sorted constexpr array with binary search' — one clause), Ch. 25 L127/L129 (phrases); `Eytzinger|branchless search|Khuong|Morin|sorting network|cache-oblivious|van Emde Boas|S-tree|bitonic|SIMD scan|vectori[sz]ed search|small array` -> 0 files; `std::sort|pdqsort|insertion sort` -> only std::sort used to sort latency samples in harness code (Ch. 1/23/24/26/42/43/46) and to make a branch predictable in Ch. 13 L88. Ch. 13 §13.4.1/§13.5 (L127-222) develops cmov vs branch in general and §13.6 L228-229 the cmov-lengthens-dependency-chain trap, but never applies it to a search loop. Ch. 10: `binary|both halves|search|tree` -> only L246, a reference entry on B-tree prefetching; no prefetched-search technique. Ch. 11: `dependent load|pointer.chas|search` -> 0 hits. Ch. 29: `sort|minps|maxps|compare.*exchange` -> only L118's generic min/max horizontal reduce; no sorting network or small-array SIMD key search (Ex. 5 L186 is a delimiter scan).

## G91 — Position, P&L, fee and FX arithmetic in scaled integers: average entry price rounding rules, realized/unrealized P&L, scale management across price × qty × fee × FX, __int128 and _Decimal64 measured, bit-exact replay

- **Theme:** L. Algorithms & numerics · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** algorithms-numerics#8
- **Verdict:** partially-covered · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** A money- and compliance-correctness topic that sits in the pre-trade risk check and the deterministic core, but the mechanisms (scaled integers, rounding as a rule, __int128, checked arithmetic) are already taught in Ch. 27 and Ch. 72 — what is missing is the applied composition, which is valuable but specialised and sits on the correctness axis rather than the latency axis the book is organised around.
- **Refined placement:** Section inside Ch. 27, as the analyst proposed: new §27.4.4 'Position, P&L, fees and FX as integer arithmetic' after §27.4.3, plus one §27.3 measurement block (the same fill -> position/avg-price/realized-P&L update in int64, __int128, double and _Decimal64 via GCC's libdfp/-mdecimal, which also discharges §27.2.1's unmeasured 'too slow' claim), two §27.6 pitfalls (average-entry-price rounding drift across many fills; fee scale finer than price scale silently truncated), one §27.7 exercise, cross-refs to Ch. 72 §72.4.3 (checked ops) and Ch. 74 §74.2.2 (bit-exact replay of the position state). Update the README outline for Ch. 27. About 900-1,300 words; no new chapter.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** In scope: signed position and the fill-update state machine; average entry price as a division with an explicit, tested rounding rule and the realized/unrealized split on partial closes and flips; scale composition (price scale x qty scale -> notional scale; fees/rebates at a finer scale such as 0.0030/share; per-venue fee schedules as constexpr tables, Ch. 18); FX conversion for multi-currency books with the rate's own scale and rounding point; __int128 intermediates and when a 128-bit product is actually emitted (one mul + imul/adc — verify in §27.5); mark-to-market against mid/last as the one place double is acceptable off the decision path; bit-exact replay because every operation is integer and ordered (ties Ch. 74). Out of scope: accounting/back-office decimal libraries beyond the one _Decimal64 measurement, and strong-type design (that is G-item algorithms-numerics#9 / Ch. 19).
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.2.1 L24-29 (scaled int vs decimal vs binary; decimal FP 'too slow' unmeasured)
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.2.2 L33-38 (tick rounding; rounding direction as a business rule)
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.4.1 L124-131 (price x qty -> __int128 notional, double-scaling, divide-with-rounding, 'P&L = Sigma(price x qty)')
  - 27-fixed-point-floating-point-arithmetic.md §27.4.3 and §27.6 L185-199 (FP determinism for replay; overflow and mixed-scale pitfalls)
  - 72-secure-programming-low-latency.md §72.4.3 L94-100 (checked arithmetic on notional/position/exposure)
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.2.1 L36 and 76-production-profiling-end-to-end-case-study.md L50 (risk/position check as a stage, policy only)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Ch. 27 develops the price representation and the primitives: §27.2.1 (L24-29) scaled int64 vs decimal FP ('slow, often software-emulated ... too slow for the hot path' — asserted, never measured) vs binary float; §27.2.2 (L33-38) tick rounding as exact integer ops and 'rounding direction is a rule, not a default'; §27.4.1 (L124-131) 'price x qty into a wider notional integer', double-scaling of two scaled values, division needs explicit rounding, __int128 intermediates with overflow check, and the single phrase 'P&L = Sigma(price x qty)' (L128); §27.4.3 FP determinism for bit-exact replay; §27.6 L195-196 mixing scales and int64 overflow pitfalls; §27.7 Ex. 2 builds a Price type with __int128 notional. Ch. 72 §72.4.3 (L94-100) adds checked arithmetic (__builtin_mul_overflow, C++26 saturating ops) on 'notional, position, exposure' for the pre-trade risk check. Ch. 74 L36 names the risk process ('limits, position, kill-switch') and Ch. 76 L50 budgets 'limit/position/kill-switch check' at ~40 ns, both as policy, no arithmetic. Greps I ran: `p&l|pnl|profit and loss|realized|unrealized|realised|average entry|entry price|cost basis|mark.to.market` -> Ch. 27 L15 ('P&L aggregation ... legitimately use double') and L128 (the one phrase), Ch. 19 L158 ('P&L vectors' as an expression-template example), Ch. 29 L15, Ch. 75 L17, Ch. 76 L89/101/113/124 (backtest P&L as an outcome) — no position/P&L state machine anywhere; `\bfee\b|\bfees\b|rebate|\bFX\b|currency conversion|multi-currency` -> only App. F L18 and App. J L15 maker-taker definitions and Ch. 41 L51 'direct fee' (unrelated); `_Decimal64|_Decimal128|decimal64|decimal128` -> 0 files; `__int128|int128` -> Ch. 27 L129/L196/L204/L211/L218 only (as an intermediate type, no measurement); `notional` -> Ch. 27 and Ch. 72 (overflow-headroom context only); `position` in Ch. 74/72/76/App. J -> risk-limit policy mentions only.

## G92 — Compression for capture, replay and bandwidth-constrained links (LZ4/zstd/dictionaries measured on tick data, delta-of-delta timestamps, columnar framing, decompression-bound replay) and RNG for simulation and benchmarks (xoshiro/PCG/Philox, O(1) distributions, reproducible parallel streams)

- **Theme:** L. Algorithms & numerics · **Proposed kind:** new-section-in-existing-chapter · **Sources:** algorithms-numerics#11, algorithms-numerics#12
- **Verdict:** genuinely-missing · **Priority:** medium
- **Why this priority:** The compression half is a real, measurable C++/Linux engineering decision (LZ4 vs zstd-with-dictionary sets retention cost and replay throughput for the capture/research tier the book already devotes Ch. 75 to) but it is off the tick-to-trade path and specialised; the RNG half on its own would be low — benchmark-harness hygiene plus Monte-Carlo stream reproducibility, marginal to the book's latency theme — so the merged item lands at medium with the recommendation to split and give the two halves different homes.
- **Refined placement:** Split the merged item back into its two unrelated halves; neither needs a chapter. (a) Compression -> Ch. 75 new §75.4.6 'Compression: codecs, domain encodings and decompression-bound replay' after §75.4.5, with one §75.3 table addition (none vs LZ4 vs zstd-1/-9 vs zstd with a trained dictionary on a captured ITCH session: ratio, compress MB/s on a housekeeping core, decompress GB/s and the resulting replay messages/s) and one §75.5 pitfall ('compressing on the capture path / replay bottlenecked on decompression'); a one-line cross-ref each from Appendix K.3 (microwave bandwidth) and Ch. 53 §53.2.1 (FAST as the protocol-level version). About 700-1,000 words. (b) RNG -> Appendix L new §L.5 'Generating inputs: PRNG choice, seeding, and keeping the generator out of the timed region' (renumber current L.5/L.6 References/Additional Reading to L.6/L.7), about 400-600 words, since every harness in the book uses an unexplained std::mt19937 and Ch. 3's own method depends on unpredictable inputs; plus two sentences in Ch. 67 §67.4.3 (cuRAND/Philox counter-based per-path streams for reproducible parallel Monte-Carlo) and a cross-ref in Ch. 74 §74.2.2 (the seed as a replayable input). Update README outlines for Ch. 75 and Appendix L.
- **What is missing / scope notes:** Compression half: measure, don't survey — LZ4 (GB/s decode, ~2-3x on raw ITCH), zstd levels and --fast, zstd dictionaries trained on short records (the big win for small fixed messages), zlib as baseline; domain encodings before the codec (delta/delta-of-delta timestamps a la Gorilla, varint/zigzag deltas, columnar framing of captured fields tying Ch. 8 SoA); framing so segments stay seekable (block size vs ratio, index checkpoints tying §75.4.4); decompression-bound replay and why decode speed, not ratio, sets research throughput; CPU placement on a housekeeping core (Ch. 42) and never on the capture ring writer's critical drain. Skip DPU compression offload beyond a cross-ref to Ch. 70. RNG half: ns/draw and state size for mt19937_64 vs xoshiro256**/PCG64/splitmix64/wyrand; rdrand/rdseed cost (hundreds of cycles, never on the hot path); why std::uniform_int_distribution and normal_distribution are not O(1) (rejection sampling, Marsaglia two-value cache) and Lemire's nearly-divisionless bounded integers; pre-generating inputs outside the timed loop (the Appendix L.1 discipline); seeding discipline and reproducibility across libraries (Ch. 1 L191's caveat generalised); counter-based generators (Philox) for reproducible parallel streams in Ch. 67/68 Monte-Carlo; the engine's own randomness (backoff jitter Ch. 32, order randomisation) passed in as a captured input so Ch. 74 replay stays bit-exact. Keep both halves measurement-led; neither justifies a chapter.
- **Closest existing coverage:**
  - 75-capture-persistence-replay-storage.md §75.4.2 L81 and §75.4.4 L100 (two sentences: 'optionally compress off-path', 'merge/compress sealed segments')
  - 53-zero-copy-wire-market-data-decoding.md §53.2.1 L29-31 (FAST as protocol-level compression, decode cost only)
  - appendix-K-exchange-connectivity.md K.3 L30 (microwave bandwidth constraint, no technique)
  - 74-process-topology-deterministic-state-machine.md §74.2.2 L50/L56 (RNG as an input to pass into the core, policy only)
  - 32-spinlocks-backoff-contention-control.md §32.4.2 L143 ('optionally add randomization (jitter)', no generator)
  - 67-gpu-computing-cuda.md §67.4.3 L113 (Monte-Carlo named, no RNG)
  - Benchmark harness code using std::mt19937 in Ch. 1, 2, 3, 7, 10, 13, 14, 15, 16, 25, 28 (used, never discussed)
- **Evidence (greps and reads):** Compression: grep -il `\blz4\b|zstd|zstandard|snappy|zlib|deflate|brotli|\blzo\b|gorilla|delta.of.delta|varint|LEB128|zigzag|dictionary compress` -> 0 files. grep -n -i `compress` -> Ch. 75 L81 ('Optionally compress off-path ... never on the capture path — and often after the capture is durable, during compaction' — one sentence) and L100 ('compaction (merge/compress sealed journal segments off-path)' — one clause); Ch. 53 L29/L31 and App. F L13 / App. J L59 describe FAST as a protocol-level compression and only as a decode burden; Ch. 70 L38/L97/L131 list compression as a DPU lookaside-offload category; Ch. 28 L26/L122 use 'compress' for pext bit-gathering; Ch. 74 L135 is about core-dump size. Appendix K L30 says microwave has 'far lower bandwidth' so 'only the most latency-critical, compact signals go over it' but names no compression technique. Ch. 75 §75.3 table and §75.5 pitfalls discuss ring sizing and disk rate, never codec ratio or decompression throughput; Ch. 75 §75.4.5 replay never mentions being decompression-bound. RNG: grep -il `xoshiro|xorshift|xoroshiro|\bpcg\b|PCG64|splitmix|philox|counter-based|wyrand|lehmer|rdrand|rdseed|Lemire bounded|ziggurat|box.muller|marsaglia|curand` -> 0 files. `mt19937|mersenne|random_device|uniform_int_distribution|normal_distribution` -> 25 hits, every one of them harness code (std::mt19937/mt19937_64 seeded with a constant in Ch. 1 L131-145, Ch. 2 L124, Ch. 3 L101-130, Ch. 7 L125, Ch. 10 L103, Ch. 13 L86, Ch. 14 L103, Ch. 15 L93, Ch. 16 L90, Ch. 25 L79-82, Ch. 28 L51) with no discussion of generator cost, state size, distributions or seeding; Ch. 1 L191 notes lognormal_distribution is not bit-specified across libraries (one parenthetical). Ch. 74 L50 lists 'RNG' among non-deterministic edge inputs and L56 says the core 'uses no randomness ... a random seed ... is passed in as part of the input' (two lines of policy). Ch. 32 §32.4.2 L143 says 'optionally add randomization (jitter)' with no generator. Ch. 67 §67.4.3 L113 and Ex. 5 L139, Ch. 68 L36/L132 discuss Monte-Carlo with no RNG, stream or reproducibility mention. Ch. 3 L33/L261/L282 argue for random, production-like inputs but never for how to generate them cheaply or outside the timed region. Appendix L (read in full, L.1-L.6) has no input-generation section.
